On the other hand, if at some iteration
\lVert Qv\rVert^{2}>1-\gamma^{2}/2
for all unit vectors
v
that satisfy
{\mathcal{A}}
, then each such
v
has inner product at least
1-\gamma^{2}/2
with some unit vector in the subspace associated with
Q
, so each such
v
has distance at most
\gamma
from some unit vector in the subspace associated with
Q
.
This is the desired guarantee.

Finally, we show that with high probability there exists an iteration of the for loop at which there exists no degree-
t
pseudo-expectation that satisfies
\mathcal{A}\cup\{\lVert Qv\rVert^{2}\leq 1-\gamma^{2}/2\}
.
Call an iteration good if the algorithm samples a unit vector
w
with
{\lVert Pw\rVert^{2}\geq 1-\sqrt{\varepsilon}}
and
\lVert Qw\rVert^{2}\leq 1-\gamma^{2}/4
.
We start by upper bounding the number of good iterations of the algorithm.
In a good iteration,
w
is
\sqrt{2}\varepsilon^{1/4}
-close to the subspace associated with
P
and
\gamma/2
-far from the subspace associated with
Q
.
Let
z
be the projection of
w
onto the subspace associated with
P
.
Then
z
is
(\gamma/2-\sqrt{2}\varepsilon^{1/4})
-far from the subspace associated with
Q
, so it is also
(\gamma/2-\sqrt{2}\varepsilon^{1/4})
-far from any
w
produced in a previous good iteration, so by the triangle inequality it is also
(\gamma/2-2\sqrt{2}\varepsilon^{1/4})
-far from any
z
produced in a previous good iteration.
We bound
\gamma/2-2\sqrt{2}\varepsilon^{1/4}\geq\gamma/4
using that
\gamma^{2}\geq 128\varepsilon^{1/2}
.
Then the number of good iterations is bounded by the size of a packing of the subspace associated with
P
with balls of radius
\gamma/4
, which is at most
O(1/\gamma)^{r}
.
To finish, we upper bound the number of bad iterations.
An iteration is bad with probability at most
\varepsilon^{1/4}
, so then by standard concentration bounds with high probability the number of bad iterations is bounded by the number of good iterations.
Therefore with high probability there exists an iteration among the first
O(1/\gamma)^{r}
ones where the algorithm cannot find a degree-
t
pseudo-expectation that satisfies
\mathcal{A}\cup\{\lVert Qv\rVert^{2}\leq 1-\gamma^{2}/2\}
, and as we argued this implies that with high probability the guarantees of the theorem hold.
∎

4.2
Proof of
Theorem

4.2

We first state a lemma that, given the system of polynomial constraints
{\mathcal{A}}
, shows that we can construct a system of polynomial constraints
{\mathcal{B}}
over unit vectors such that (1) if a vector satisfies
{\mathcal{B}}
, then it is nearly contained in the subspace described by
R
, and (2) if a vector is nearly contained in one of the subspaces described by
P_{1},\ldots,P_{k}
, then it satisfies
{\mathcal{B}}
.
Then the proof of
Theorem

4.2
consists of applying the rounding algorithm in
Theorem

4.1
to the system
{\mathcal{B}}
.

Lemma 4.3

(Sum-of-squares orthogonal complement)
.

There is an algorithm that takes input a system of polynomial inequalities
\mathcal{A}(v)=\{\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1,p_{1}(v)\geq 0,\ldots,p_{m}(v)\geq 0\}
in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and outputs a system of polynomial inequalities
\mathcal{B}(u)
in indeterminate
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with the following guarantee:

Suppose there exist orthogonal projection matrices
P_{1},P_{2},\ldots,P_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that
\mathcal{A}(v)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.26668pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\{\|P_{i}v\|^{2}\leq\varepsilon\}
for all
i\in[k]
and for some
\varepsilon<1
small enough.
Suppose further that there exists an orthogonal projection matrix
R\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
of rank
r
such that

\left\{\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1,Rv=0\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.26668pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}(v)\,.

Then the algorithm runs in time
(dm)^{O(t)}
and outputs a system of polynomial inequalities
\mathcal{B}(u)
of size
(dm)^{O(t)}
in indeterminate
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that

1.

\mathcal{B}(u)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.728pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.728pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.728pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.728pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.728pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\{\lVert u\rVert^{2}=1\}
,

2.

\mathcal{B}(u)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{10.92361pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.92361pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[10.92361pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t^{2}}$}}}\hskip-10.92361pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[10.92361pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\{\|Ru\|^{2}\geq 1-4\varepsilon\}
,

3.

All unit vectors
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\lVert P_{i}w\rVert^{2}\geq 1-\varepsilon
for some
i\in[k]
are feasible for
\mathcal{B}(w)
.

Furthermore, if in each sum-of-squares proof
{\mathcal{A}(v)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.26668pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\{\|P_{i}v\|^{2}\leq\varepsilon\}}
each monomial
r(v)^{2}\prod_{i\in S}p_{i}(v)
has
|S|\leq q
, then
\mathcal{B}(u)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{27.3122pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-27.3122pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[27.3122pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t(q+1)}$}}}\hskip-27.3122pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[27.3122pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\{\|Ru\|^{2}\geq 1-4\varepsilon\}
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{B}(u)
be the following system of polynomial inequalities in indeterminate
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

1.

\lVert u\rVert^{2}=1
,

2.

There exists a degree-
t
sum-of-squares proof in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

{\mathcal{A}}(v)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.26668pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\{\langle v,u\rangle^{2}\leq 4\varepsilon\right\}\,.

We note that the second constraint can be encoded with
(dm)^{O(t)}
polynomial inequalities:
see for example the discussion about succinct representations in Section 4.3.4 in
[
25
]
.

We now show that
{\mathcal{B}}(u)
has the desired properties. Property (1) is immediate from the first constraints in
{\mathcal{B}}(u)
.

For property (2), because
\left\lVert I_{d}-R\right\rVert\leq 1
we have by
Fact

3.13
that
{\mathcal{B}}(u)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.728pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.728pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.728pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.728pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.728pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\left\{\left\lVert(I_{d}-R)u\right\rVert^{2}\leq\left\lVert u\right\rVert^{2}=1\right\}
.
Further, because
R^{2}=R
we have that
R(I_{d}-R)=0
and thus
\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.728pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.728pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.728pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{0}$}}}\hskip-8.728pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.728pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\left\{R(I_{d}-R)u=0\right\}
.
Therefore, because
\left\{\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1,Rv=0\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.26668pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}(v)
, by replacing
v
by
(I_{d}-R)u
we get that
{{\mathcal{B}}(u)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.728pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.728pt\raisebox{-6.26668pt}{\makebox[8.728pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\hskip-8.728pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.728pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}((I_{d}-R)u)}
.
By composition with the second constraint in
{\mathcal{B}}(u)
, we get that
\mathcal{B}(u)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{10.92361pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.92361pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[10.92361pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t^{2}}$}}}\hskip-10.92361pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[10.92361pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\left\{\langle u,(I_{d}-R)u\rangle^{2}\leq 4\varepsilon\right\}
.
We can rewrite this as
\mathcal{B}(u)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{10.92361pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.92361pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[10.92361pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t^{2}}$}}}\hskip-10.92361pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[10.92361pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\left\{\lVert Ru\rVert^{2}\geq 1-4\varepsilon\right\}
, as desired.

Finally, for property (3), let
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a unit vector such that
\lVert P_{i}w\rVert^{2}\geq 1-\varepsilon
for some
i\in[k]
.
Then

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(v)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.26668pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\langle v,w\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=\langle P_{i}v+(I_{d}-P_{i})v,w\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\langle P_{i}v,w\rangle^{2}+2\langle(I_{d}-P_{i})v,w\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle=2\langle P_{i}v,w\rangle^{2}+2\langle v,(I_{d}-P_{i})w\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 4\varepsilon\,,

where in the first inequality we used
Fact

3.8
and in the last inequality we used Cauchy-Schwarz (
Fact

3.7
) to bound the first term in the sum using that
{\mathcal{A}}(v)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.26668pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\{\lVert P_{i}v\rVert^{2}\leq\varepsilon\right\}
and the second term in the sum using that
\lVert(I_{d}-P_{i})w\rVert^{2}\leq\varepsilon
.
Therefore,
w
is feasible for
\mathcal{B}(w)
.

Furthermore, suppose in each sum-of-squares proof
{\mathcal{A}(v)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.26668pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\{\|P_{i}v\|^{2}\leq\varepsilon\}}
each monomial
r(v)^{2}\prod_{i\in S}p_{i}(v)
has
|S|\leq q
.
Note that in this case in the sum-of-squares proof that we give in the proof of property (3) each monomial
r(v)^{2}\prod_{i\in S}p_{i}(v)
also has
|S|\leq q
.
Therefore we can require the same of the sum-of-squares proof in the second constraint of
{\mathcal{B}}(u)
.
Then this implies by composition that the sum-of-squares proof
\mathcal{B}(u)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.728pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.728pt\raisebox{-1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.728pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-8.728pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.728pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\{\|Ru\|^{2}\geq 1-4\varepsilon\}
has degree at most
t(q+1)
.
∎

Proof of
Theorem

4.2
.

By
Lemma

4.3
, there is an algorithm that runs in time
(dm)^{O(t)}
and constructs a system of polynomial inequalities
\mathcal{B}
of size
(dm)^{O(t)}
in indeterminate unit vectors
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\mathcal{B}(u)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{10.92361pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-10.92361pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[10.92361pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t^{2}}$}}}\hskip-10.92361pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[10.92361pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\{\|Ru\|^{2}\geq 1-4\varepsilon\}
and such that all unit vectors
w
with
\lVert P_{i}w\rVert^{2}\geq 1-\varepsilon
for some
i\in[n]
are feasible for
{\mathcal{B}}(w)
.
Then the result follows by applying the algorithm in
Theorem

4.1
to
{\mathcal{B}}
, leading to time complexity
(dm)^{O(t^{3})}\cdot O(1/\gamma)^{r}
.

Furthermore, if in each sum-of-squares proof
{\mathcal{A}(v)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.26668pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\{\|P_{i}v\|^{2}\leq\varepsilon\}}
each monomial
r(v)^{2}\prod_{i\in S}p_{i}(v)
has
|S|\leq q
, then
Lemma

4.3
implies that
\mathcal{B}(u)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{27.3122pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-27.3122pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[27.3122pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{t(q+1)}$}}}\hskip-27.3122pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[27.3122pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\{\|Ru\|^{2}\geq 1-4\varepsilon\}
, and then
Theorem

4.1
leads to time complexity
(dm)^{O(t^{2}(q+1))}\cdot O(1/\gamma)^{r}
.
∎

5
Clustering Mixtures of Centered Gaussians

In this section, we show how our sum-of-squares based dimension reduction method yields significantly improved algorithms for outlier-robustly clustering mixtures of well-separated centered Gaussians.
Our notion of separation is in a distance (studied in prior works such as
[
6
,
16
]
) natural for non-spherical mixtures, which for centered Gaussian components corresponds to total variation separation of
1-f(1/w_{\min})
where
w_{\min}
is the minimum mixing weight of any component in the input mixture (see
Fact

3.17
).

Definition 5.1

(Centered parameter distance)
.

We say that two centered distributions with covariances
\Sigma_{1},\Sigma_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
are
\Delta
-separated if at least one of the following conditions hold for
\{i,j\}=\{1,2\}
:

•

Spectral separation:
\exists v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v>\Delta^{2}\cdot v^{\top}\Sigma_{j}v
,

•

Relative Frobenius separation:
\left\lVert\Sigma_{i}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{j}\Sigma_{i}^{-1/2}-I_{d}\right\rVert^{2}_{F}>\Delta^{2}\cdot\left\lVert\Sigma_{i}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{j}\Sigma_{i}^{-1/2}\right\rVert^{2}
.

Our algorithm has sample and time complexity that are
fixed polynomials
in the underlying dimension
d
.
The best-known prior works require samples and time

\gg d^{O(k)}

[
3
]
.
Importantly, unlike prior work
[
6
]
, our algorithm
does not need
certifiable anti-concentration properties of the component distributions, and indeed this is the main technical reason why we obtain a fixed polynomial (as opposed to
d^{\mathrm{poly}(k)}
) sample and time complexity.

Theorem 5.2

(Main theorem, centered components)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a
d
-dimensional mixture of
k
Gaussians
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}N(0,\Sigma_{i})
with
w_{\min}=\min_{i}w_{i}
and
\Sigma_{1},\ldots,\Sigma_{k}\succ 0
.
Furthermore, assume all components
i\neq j
are
\Delta
-separated
.
Also let
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
be a distribution satisfying
d_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime},\mathcal{M})\leq\varepsilon^{*}
.
Suppose
\Delta,(\varepsilon^{*})^{-1}\geq f(w_{\min}^{-1})
for some function
f
.
Then, given
\mathrm{poly}(d,w_{\min}^{-1},\Delta)
i.i.d. samples from
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, there exists an algorithm that runs in time
g(w_{\min}^{-1},\Delta)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(d)
for some function
g
and outputs with high probability a partition of the samples
\hat{S}_{1},\ldots,\hat{S}_{k}
such that, if we let
S_{i}
be the set of samples generated according to the
i
-th component, then with high probability (up to a permutation of
\hat{S}_{1},\ldots,\hat{S}_{k}
)
12
12

12

It suffices to take
f(x)=\exp(\exp(\tilde{O}(x^{2})))
and
g(x,y)=\exp(\exp(\exp(\exp(\tilde{O}(\min(x,y)^{2})))))
.

\min_{i}\frac{|\hat{S}_{i}\cap S_{i}|}{|S_{i}|}\geq 1-O(kw_{\min}^{-1}\max\{\varepsilon^{*},\Delta^{-\Omega(1/k)}\})\,.

Remark 5.3
.

By a recent result of Blanc and Valiant
[
10
]
that proved an equivalence between oblivious and adaptive adversaries, we can also generalize
Theorem

5.2
to the adaptive adversary setting.
In the adaptive adversary setting, a set
X
of
\mathrm{poly}(d,w_{\min}^{-1},\Delta)
i.i.d. samples is drawn from
\mathcal{M}
, and then the input consists of an arbitrary set of samples
Y
with
|Y|=|X|
such that
|Y\cap X|\geq(1-\varepsilon^{*})|X|
.
We need to appeal to their result because, otherwise, in the adaptive adversary setting we could not guarantee that the samples used in the clustering selection step are independent of the samples used to construct the candidate clusterings; this difficulty also appears in
[
3
]
.
To apply
[
10
]
, we would discretize our data and then apply their Theorem 2, as described in their Remark 2.
Because their result only incurs a loss in sample complexity proportional to the
logarithm
of the size of the domain, we can afford a domain size of
(1/\delta)^{d}
, even with
\delta
exponentially small in
d
, while only incurring a loss in sample complexity polynomial in
d
.

Corollary 5.4

(Subspace clustering)
.

In the same setting as
Theorem

5.2
, if all components
i\neq j
have
\operatorname{colspan}(\Sigma_{i})\neq\operatorname{colspan}(\Sigma_{j})
, then the guarantees of
Theorem

5.2
hold with any
\Delta\geq f(w_{\min}^{-1})
for some function
f
.
13
13

13

We stated
Theorem

5.2
for positive definite covariance matrices, but we can reduce to that case by convolving all samples with i.i.d. Gaussians
N(0,\delta I_{d})
for a
\delta
that is inverse exponentially small in
n
.

We prove
Theorem

5.2
in
Section

5.2.2
, and we give here a short overview of the proof.

If two components of the mixture have relative Frobenius separation, then an algorithm of
[
3
]
partially clusters the mixture with samples and time
f(w_{\min}^{-1})\cdot\mathrm{poly}(d)
.
This allows us to assume that there is no relative Frobenius separation and therefore that all components of the mixture are spectrally separated.
In this setting, we give an algorithm that finds directions in which the mixture is spectrally separated.
Finally, we partially cluster the mixture along these directions, and we iterate these steps until we obtain
k
clusters.

The bulk of our work is in
Section

5.1
, where we recover a low-dimensional subspace containing directions of spectral separation.
Then in
Section

5.2
we use this subspace-finding subroutine to obtain an algorithm that satisfies the guarantees of
Theorem

5.2
.

5.1
Finding Directions with Spectral Separation

In this section we show how to recover directions with spectral separation when the components do not have Frobenius separation.
When the mixture is in the isotropic position and has lower bounded mixing weights, the directions of spectral separation are roughly equivalent to the directions in which one component has very small variance.
Thus, we phrase our guarantees in terms of recovering directions in which at least one component has small variance.

Our algorithms will only be able to ensure the following relaxation of isotropy that will still be enough for our purposes.

Definition 5.5

(
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position, centered components)
.

For
\gamma\leq 1
, a centered mixture is in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position if
(1-\gamma)I_{d}\preceq\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\Sigma_{i}\preceq(1+\gamma)I_{d}
and
{\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\Sigma_{i}-I_{d}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\gamma}
. We say that the mixture is (exactly) isotropic when
\gamma=0
.

Theorem 5.6

(Subspace finding theorem, centered components)
.

Consider a
d
-dimensional mixture of
k
Gaussians
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}N(0,\Sigma_{i})
with
w_{\min}=\min_{i}w_{i}
and
\Sigma_{1},\ldots,\Sigma_{k}\succ 0
in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position.
Suppose
\lVert\Sigma_{i}-I_{d}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq r
for all
i\in[k]
, where
r\geq 1
.

Let
\varepsilon>0
with
\varepsilon\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{16})
, and suppose
\gamma\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{4}\varepsilon^{2})
.
Then, given
\mathrm{poly}(d,w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1})
samples from the mixture with an
\varepsilon
-fraction of corruptions, there exists an algorithm that runs in time
f(w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1},r)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(d)
and outputs an orthogonal projection matrix
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
with
\mathrm{rank}(Q)\leq g(w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1},r)
such that with high probability, for every unit vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{-4}\varepsilon^{1/128})
for some
i\in[k]
, there exists a unit vector
v^{\prime}
such that
Qv^{\prime}=v^{\prime}
and
\lVert v-v^{\prime}\rVert\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{1/1024})
.

Proof outline.
The proof of
Theorem

5.6
has three main components:

1.

In
Section

5.1.1
we construct a system of polynomial inequalities
\mathcal{A}
from the
exact
fourth moments of the mixture that identifies the intersection of the eigenspaces of
\Sigma_{i}
that have eigenvalues
\approx 1
. Specifically, every vector that satisfies
\mathcal{A}
is close for all
i\in[k]
to one eigenvector of
\Sigma_{i}
with eigenvalue
\approx 1
, and in the converse, every vector that is simultaneously for all
i\in[k]
an eigenvector of
\Sigma_{i}
with eigenvalue
\approx 1
satisfies
\mathcal{A}
.

2.

In
Section

5.1.2
we show we can construct a system of polynomial inequalities
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
from the
approximate
fourth moments of the mixture with the same properties as
{\mathcal{A}}
.

3.

In
Section

5.1.3
we argue that
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies the conditions of the rounding algorithm analyzed in
Theorem

4.2
.

We will address these steps and then return to the proof of
Theorem

5.6
.
We start by defining the polynomials that form the basis of our system of constraints.

Definition 5.7
.

We define the following homogeneous polynomials in
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
whose coefficient tensors are functions of
w_{1},\ldots,w_{k}
and
\Sigma_{1},\ldots,\Sigma_{k}
:

•

p(v)=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v)^{2}
,

•

p^{\prime\prime}(v)=8\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(\Sigma_{i}v)^{\otimes 2}+4\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v)\Sigma_{i}
,

•

q(v)=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)^{2}
.

Lemma 5.8
.

Let
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be distributed according to the ground truth mixture.
Suppose the mixture is in exact isotropic position.
Then

•

p(v)=\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\bm{x}^{\otimes 4},v^{\otimes 4}\rangle/3=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}/3
,

•

u^{\top}p^{\prime\prime}(v)u=4\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\bm{x}^{\otimes 4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle
,

•

q(v)=\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\bm{x}^{\otimes 4}-I_{d}^{\otimes 2},v^{\otimes 4}\rangle/3=\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}/3-\lVert v\rVert^{4}
.

Proof.

The first claim follows from the fact that the fourth moment of a 1-D Gaussian random variable
N(0,\sigma^{2})
is
3\sigma^{4}
.
For the second claim, we use that
p^{\prime\prime}(v)
is the Hessian of
p(v)
to obtain

p^{\prime\prime}(v)=(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}/3)^{\prime\prime}=(4\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{3}\bm{x}/3)^{\prime}=4\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2}\bm{x}\bm{x}^{\top}\,,

so

u^{\top}p^{\prime\prime}(v)u=4\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2}\langle\bm{x},u\rangle^{2}=4\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\bm{x}^{\otimes 4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle\,.

For the third claim,

q(v)=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v)^{2}-2\|v\|^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v)+\|v\|^{4}\,,

so using that
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\Sigma_{i}=I_{d}
, we have

q(v)=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v)^{2}-\|v\|^{4}=p(v)-\lVert v\rVert^{4}\,.

∎

5.1.1
Identifying Eigenspaces with Eigenvalues
\approx 1
Using Exact Moments

Definition 5.9
.

Let
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be distributed according to the ground truth mixture.
We define
\mathcal{A}(v,\varepsilon)
to be the following system of polynomial inequalities in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

1.

\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
,

2.

\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}/3-\lVert v\rVert^{4}\right)^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{8}
,

3.

There exists a degree-
O(1)
sum-of-squares proof in indeterminate
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\left\{\left(4\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\bm{x}^{\otimes 4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle-4\lVert v\rVert^{2}\lVert u\rVert^{2}-8\langle v,u\rangle^{2}\right)^{2}\leq\varepsilon(\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\lVert u\rVert^{8})\right\}\,.

See
Lemma

5.10
and the discussion in the paragraph succeeding it for some intuition about the second and third constraints.
We note that the third constraint can be encoded with
\mathrm{poly}(d)
polynomial inequalities:
see, for example, the discussion about succinct representations in Section 4.3.4 in
[
25
]
.

Lemma 5.10
.

Suppose the mixture is in exact isotropic position.
Then
{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)
is equivalent to the following system of polynomial inequalities in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

1.

\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
,

2.

q(v)^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{8}
,

3.

There exists a degree-
O(1)
sum-of-squares proof in indeterminate
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\left\{\left(u^{\top}\left(p^{\prime\prime}(v)-4\|v\|^{2}I_{d}-8v^{\otimes 2}\right)u\right)^{2}\leq\varepsilon\left(\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\lVert u\rVert^{8}\right)\right\}\,.

Proof.

The claims follow from
Lemma

5.8
.
∎

The third constraint is intended to impose an upper bound on the spectral norm of the matrix
p^{\prime\prime}(v)-4\|v\|^{2}I_{d}-8v^{\otimes 2}
. However, the spectral norm of a matrix is not a polynomial in its entries, so we cannot impose such a constraint directly. Instead, we impose the stronger constraint that there exists a sum-of-squares proof in some indeterminate
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
that the quadratic
u^{\top}(p^{\prime\prime}(v)-4\|v\|^{2}I_{d}-8v^{\otimes 2})u
is bounded.
Note that such a constraint implies a spectral norm bound on the desired matrix.

For ease of notation, we write
{\mathcal{A}}
without
v
or
\varepsilon
when understood from context.

We show now that
{\mathcal{A}}(v)
implies that
v
is approximately an eigenvector of eigenvalue
\approx 1
for all
\Sigma_{i}
s.

Lemma 5.11

(Identifiability of approximate eigenvectors)
.

Suppose the mixture is in exact isotropic position.
Then for all
i\in[k]

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\{\|(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v\|^{2}\leq w_{\min}^{-3}\varepsilon^{1/16}\right\}.

Proof.

We start by observing that the constraint
q(v)^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{8}
, which is equivalent to

{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)^{2}\right)^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{8}\,,

also implies that, for all
i\in[k]
,
{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}w_{i}^{2}(v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)^{4}\leq\varepsilon^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{8}
.

Let us turn to the third constraint. By
Fact

3.10
and
Fact

3.8
we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v,u}$}}}$}}}

\displaystyle\left(u^{\top}\left(8\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(\Sigma_{i}v)^{\otimes 2}+4\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v)\Sigma_{i}-4\|v\|^{2}I_{d}-8v^{\otimes 2}\right)u\right)^{4}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon^{2}\left(\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\lVert u\rVert^{8}\right)^{2}\leq 2\varepsilon^{2}\left(\lVert v\rVert^{16}+\lVert u\rVert^{16}\right)

and then again by
Fact

3.8
we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v,u}$}}}$}}}

\displaystyle 16\varepsilon^{2}(\lVert v\rVert^{16}+\lVert u\rVert^{16})

\displaystyle\geq\left(u^{\top}\left(8\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(\Sigma_{i}v)^{\otimes 2}-8v^{\otimes 2}\right)u\right)^{4}-8\left(u^{\top}\left(4\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v)\Sigma_{i}-4\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}\right)u\right)^{4}\,.

We now bound the second term on the right-hand side in order to obtain an upper bound on the first term on the right-hand side. We have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v,u}$}}}$}}}

\displaystyle\left(u^{\top}\left(4\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v)\Sigma_{i}-4\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}\right)u\right)^{4}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{=}}\left(u^{\top}\left(4\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)\Sigma_{i}\right)u\right)^{4}

\displaystyle=\left(4\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)(u^{\top}\Sigma_{i}u)\right)^{4}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\leq}}256k^{3}\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}^{4}(v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)^{4}\left(u^{\top}\Sigma_{i}u\right)^{4}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{\leq}}256k^{3}\varepsilon^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{8}\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}^{2}\left(u^{\top}\Sigma_{i}u\right)^{4}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(4)}}{{\leq}}O(k^{4}w_{\min}^{-2}\varepsilon^{2})\lVert v\rVert^{8}\lVert u\rVert^{8}

\displaystyle\leq O(k^{4}w_{\min}^{-2}\varepsilon^{2})\left(\lVert v\rVert^{16}+\lVert u\rVert^{16}\right)\,,

where in (1) we used that
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\Sigma_{i}=I_{d}
, in (2) we used
Fact

3.8
, in (3) we used that
{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}w_{i}^{2}(v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)^{4}\leq\varepsilon^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{8}
, in (4) we used
Fact

3.13
and that
\lVert\Sigma_{i}\rVert\leq O(w_{i}^{-1})
.
Then we conclude that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v,u}$}}}$}}}\left(u^{\top}\left(8\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(\Sigma_{i}v)^{\otimes 2}-8v^{\otimes 2}\right)u\right)^{4}

\displaystyle\leq 16\varepsilon^{2}(\lVert v\rVert^{16}+\lVert u\rVert^{16})+O(k^{4}w_{\min}^{-2}\varepsilon^{2})\left(\lVert v\rVert^{16}+\lVert u\rVert^{16}\right)

\displaystyle\leq O(k^{4}w_{\min}^{-2}\varepsilon^{2})\left(\lVert v\rVert^{16}+\lVert u\rVert^{16}\right)\,.

From now on, we aim to adjust this inequality to obtain a bound on
\lVert(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v\rVert^{2}
for all
i\in[k]
.
Replace
u
by
(\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top})u
.
An easy calculation shows that

\displaystyle u^{\top}\left(\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top}\right)\left(8\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(\Sigma_{i}v)^{\otimes 2}-8v^{\otimes 2}\right)\left(\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top}\right)u

\displaystyle\quad=u^{\top}\left(8\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\left(\left(\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top}\right)\Sigma_{i}v\right)^{\otimes 2}\right)u\,.

Then, by the bound obtained so far with
u
replaced by
(\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top})u
, and using that
{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top}\preceq\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}
, we get

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v,u}$}}}$}}}\left(u^{\top}\left(8\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\left(\left(\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top}\right)\Sigma_{i}v\right)^{\otimes 2}\right)u\right)^{4}\leq O(k^{4}w_{\min}^{-2}\varepsilon^{2})\left(\lVert v\rVert^{16}+\lVert v\rVert^{32}\lVert u\rVert^{16}\right)\,.

This also implies that, for all
i\in[k]
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v,u}$}}}$}}}\left(u^{\top}\left(8w_{i}\left(\left(\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top}\right)\Sigma_{i}v\right)^{\otimes 2}\right)u\right)^{4}\leq O(k^{4}w_{\min}^{-2}\varepsilon^{2})\left(\lVert v\rVert^{16}+\lVert v\rVert^{32}\lVert u\rVert^{16}\right)\,.

Replace
u
by
\left(\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top}\right)\Sigma_{i}v
.
Then, using that
{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
and that
{\lVert\Sigma_{i}\rVert\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1})}
implies
{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\lVert\left(\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top}\right)\Sigma_{i}v\rVert^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-2})
, we get

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\lVert\left(\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top}\right)\Sigma_{i}v\right\rVert^{16}\leq O(k^{4}w_{\min}^{-22}\varepsilon^{2})\,,

so by taking roots using
Fact

3.11
we get

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\lVert\left(\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top}\right)\Sigma_{i}v\right\rVert^{2}\leq O(k^{1/2}w_{\min}^{-11/4}\varepsilon^{1/4})\,.

Now we bound
\lVert(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v\rVert^{2}
in terms of this inequality.
Note that

\left(\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top}\right)\Sigma_{i}v=\lVert v\rVert^{2}\Sigma_{i}v-(v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v)v=\lVert v\rVert^{2}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v-(v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)v\,,

so by
Fact

3.8
and using that
{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}w_{i}(v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)^{2}\leq\varepsilon\lVert v\rVert^{4}
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\lVert\lVert v\rVert^{2}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\lVert\left(\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-vv^{\top}\right)\Sigma_{i}v\right\rVert^{2}+2(v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O(k^{1/2}w_{\min}^{-11/4}\varepsilon^{1/4})+O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\leq O(k^{1/2}w_{\min}^{-11/4}\varepsilon^{1/4})\,,

so

{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\lVert v\rVert^{4}\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v\right\rVert^{2}\leq O(k^{1/2}w_{\min}^{-11/4}\varepsilon^{1/4})\,.

Because
\lVert\Sigma_{i}-I_{d}\rVert\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1})
we have
{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\lVert v\rVert^{2}\geq O(w_{\min}^{2})\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v\right\rVert^{2}
, so by
Fact

3.10

{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\lVert v\rVert^{4}\geq O(w_{\min}^{4})\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v\right\rVert^{4}
, so combined with the previous bound

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v\right\rVert^{6}\leq O(k^{1/2}w_{\min}^{-27/4}\varepsilon^{1/4})\,.

Trivially, because
\lVert\Sigma_{i}-I_{d}\rVert\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1})
, we have by
Fact

3.13
that
{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v\right\rVert^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-2})
, so also

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v\right\rVert^{8}\leq O(k^{1/2}w_{\min}^{-35/4}\varepsilon^{1/4})\,.

so by
Fact

3.11

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v\right\rVert^{2}\leq O(k^{1/8}w_{\min}^{-35/16}\varepsilon^{1/16})\,.

Finally, this is further upper bounded by
w_{\min}^{-3}\varepsilon^{1/16}
.
∎

Next, we prove an easy corollary of
Lemma

5.11
, namely that
{\mathcal{A}}(v)
implies that
v
is close to the subspace of eigenvectors of eigenvalue
\approx 1
for all
\Sigma_{i}
s.

Lemma 5.12

(Identifiability of approximate eigenspaces)
.

Suppose the mixture is in exact isotropic position.
Let
P_{i}
be the orthogonal projection to the subspace of eigenvectors of
\Sigma_{i}
such that their eigenvalues lie outside
[1-\delta,1+\delta]
. Then for all
i\in[k]

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\{\|P_{i}v\|^{2}\leq w_{\min}^{-3}\varepsilon^{1/16}\delta^{-2}\}.

Proof.

Let
S_{-}=(-\infty,1-\delta)
and
S_{+}=(1+\delta,\infty)
. Fix some
\Sigma_{i}
and let its eigenvalue decomposition be
\Sigma_{i}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lambda_{j}s_{j}s_{j}^{\top}
. By
Lemma

5.11
,
{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\lVert(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v\rVert^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}
where
\varepsilon^{\prime}=w_{\min}^{-3}\varepsilon^{1/16}
. Then

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\lVert\sum_{j=1}^{d}(\lambda_{j}-1)s_{j}s_{j}^{\top}v\right\rVert^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\,,

which is equivalent to

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(\lambda_{j}-1)^{2}(s_{j}^{\top}v)^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\,.

Therefore we also have

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\delta^{2}\sum_{j:\lambda_{j}\in S_{-}\cup S_{+}}(s_{j}^{\top}v)^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\,,

so

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}v^{\top}\left(\sum_{j:\lambda_{j}\in S_{-}\cup S_{+}}s_{j}s_{j}^{\top}\right)\left(\sum_{j:\lambda_{j}\in S_{-}\cup S_{+}}s_{j}s_{j}^{\top}\right)v=\sum_{j:\lambda_{j}\in S_{-}\cup S_{+}}(s_{j}^{\top}v)^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}/\delta^{2}\,,

so

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\lVert P_{i}v\rVert^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}/\delta^{2}\,.

∎

We also prove the converse: if
v
is in the subspace of eigenvectors of eigenvalue
\approx 1
for all
\Sigma_{i}
s, then
v
satisfies
{\mathcal{A}}(v)
.

Lemma 5.13

(Sum-of-squares feasibility)
.

Suppose the mixture is in exact isotropic position.
Let
P_{i}
be the orthogonal projection to the subspace of eigenvectors of
\Sigma_{i}
such that their eigenvalues lie outside
[1-\delta,1+\delta]
. Let
P
be the orthogonal projection to the span of the subspaces associated with
P_{i}
for all
i\in[k]
. Then

\left\{\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1,Pv=0\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}\left(v,O(k^{2}\delta^{2})\right)\,.

Proof.

For ease of notation, let
{\mathcal{C}}=\left\{\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1,Pv=0\right\}
.
We note that, by the definition of
P
, we have
{(I_{d}-P)\Sigma_{i}(I_{d}-P)=I_{d}+E_{i}}
where
\lVert E_{i}\rVert\leq\delta
.
We will prove that
v
satisfies each of the three constraints of
{\mathcal{A}}
.
First, we have trivially that
{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\{\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1\right\}
.

Second, using that
{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\{(I_{d}-P)v=v\right\}
and
Fact

3.13
we have

{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}q(v)^{2}=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\left(v^{\top}\left(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d}\right)v\right)^{2}\right)^{2}=\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\left(v^{\top}E_{i}v\right)^{2}\right)^{2}\leq\delta^{4}\lVert v\rVert^{8}\,.

Third, let us inspect

\displaystyle p^{\prime\prime}(v)-4\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-8v^{\otimes 2}

\displaystyle=8\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(\Sigma_{i}v)^{\otimes 2}+4\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v)\Sigma_{i}-4\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-8v^{\otimes 2}\,.

For the first and fourth terms, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v,u}$}}}$}}}

\displaystyle\left(u^{\top}\left(8\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(\Sigma_{i}v)^{\otimes 2}-8v^{\otimes 2}\right)u\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(u^{\top}\left(8\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(I_{d}+(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d}))vv^{\top}(I_{d}+(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d}))-8v^{\otimes 2}\right)u\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(u^{\top}\left(8\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\left((\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})vv^{\top}+vv^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})+(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})vv^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})\right)\right)u\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(8\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\left(u^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v(v^{\top}u)+(u^{\top}v)v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})u+u^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})vv^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})u\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{\leq}}192k\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}^{2}\Big((u^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)^{2}(v^{\top}u)^{2}+(u^{\top}v)^{2}(v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})u)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+(u^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)^{2}(v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})u)^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\leq}}O\left(k\delta^{2}\right)\lVert v\rVert^{4}\lVert u\rVert^{4}\leq O\left(k\delta^{2}\left(\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\lVert u\rVert^{8}\right)\right)\,,

where in (1) we used
Fact

3.8
and in (2) we used that by
Fact

3.7
and
Fact

3.13
we have
{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v,u}$}}}$}}}(u^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v)^{2}\leq\lVert u\rVert^{2}\lVert(\Sigma_{i}-I_{d})v\rVert^{2}=\lVert u\rVert^{2}\lVert E_{i}v\rVert^{2}\leq\delta^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{2}\lVert u\rVert^{2}
. For the second and third terms, using that
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\Sigma_{i}=I_{d}
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v,u}$}}}$}}}

\displaystyle\left(u^{\top}\left(4\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v)\Sigma_{i}-4\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}\right)u\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(u^{\top}\left(4\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}(I_{d}+E_{i})v)\Sigma_{i}-4\lVert v\rVert^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\Sigma_{i}\right)u\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(u^{\top}\left(4\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}E_{i}v)\Sigma_{i}\right)u\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(4\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}E_{i}v)(u^{\top}\Sigma_{i}u)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{\leq}}16k\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}^{2}(v^{\top}E_{i}v)^{2}(u^{\top}\Sigma_{i}u)^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{\leq}}O(k^{2}\delta^{2})\lVert v\rVert^{4}\lVert u\rVert^{4}\leq O\left(k^{2}\delta^{2}\left(\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\lVert u\rVert^{8}\right)\right)\,,

where in (1) we used
Fact

3.8
and in (2) we used
Fact

3.13
and that
\lVert E_{i}\rVert\leq\delta
,
\lVert\Sigma_{i}\rVert\leq w_{i}^{-1}
. Combining the two upper bounds, we get by
Fact

3.8
that

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v,u}$}}}$}}}\left(u^{\top}\left(p^{\prime\prime}(v)-2\lVert v\rVert^{2}I_{d}-4v^{\otimes 2}\right)u\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O\left(k^{2}\delta^{2}\left(\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\lVert u\rVert^{8}\right)\right)\,.

∎

5.1.2
Identifying Eigenspaces with Eigenvalues
\approx 1
Using Approximate Moments

In this section we show how to construct a system of polynomial constraints
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
with properties similar to
{\mathcal{A}}
when only having access to approximate moments. Before doing so, we state two results on the closeness of the empirical moments to the population moments.

Fact 5.14

(Theorem 1.3 and Lemma 5.4 in
[
36
]
)
.

Given
\mathrm{poly}(d,w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1})
samples from the mixture with an
\varepsilon
-fraction of corruptions, where
\varepsilon\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{2})
, there exists an algorithm that runs in time
\mathrm{poly}(d,w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1})
and outputs symmetric tensor moment estimates
\widehat{M}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
and
\widehat{M}_{4}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{4}}
of the tensor moments of the mixture such that with high probability, for all vectors
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\langle M_{2}-\widehat{M}_{2},v^{\otimes 2}\rangle^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1}\sqrt{\varepsilon})\cdot\langle M_{2},v^{\otimes 2}\rangle^{2}

and

\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},v^{\otimes 4}\rangle^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-2}\sqrt{\varepsilon})\cdot\langle M_{2},v^{\otimes 2}\rangle^{4}\,.

Furthermore, there exist degree-
O(1)
sum-of-squares proofs in
v
of these inequalities.

We obtain the following simple corollary for mixtures in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position:

Corollary 5.15
.

Suppose the mixture is in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position. Then the same result as in
Fact

5.14
holds with upper bounds

\langle M_{2}-\widehat{M}_{2},v^{\otimes 2}\rangle^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1}\sqrt{\varepsilon})\lVert v\rVert^{4}

and

\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},v^{\otimes 4}\rangle^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-2}\sqrt{\varepsilon})\lVert v\rVert^{8}\,.

Proof.

Because of the
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position, we have that
\lVert M_{2}\rVert\leq 1+\gamma
.
Then, for any even
t\geq 2
, using
Fact

3.13
,

\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{19.56857pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-19.56857pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[19.56857pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(t)}$}}}\hskip-19.56857pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[19.56857pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\langle M_{2},v^{\otimes 2}\rangle^{t}=(v^{\top}M_{2}v)^{t}\leq\lVert M_{2}\rVert^{t}\lVert v\rVert^{2t}\leq(1+\gamma)^{t}\lVert v\rVert^{2t}\,.

Setting
t=2
and
t=4
leads to the desired conclusions.
∎

Lemma 5.16

(Empirical fourth moment Hessian bound)
.

Suppose we have some
\widehat{M}_{4}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{4}}
such that

\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\{\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},v^{\otimes 4}\rangle^{2}\leq\varepsilon\lVert v\rVert^{8}\right\}\,.

Then

\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v,u}$}}}$}}}\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle^{2}\leq O(\varepsilon(\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\lVert u\rVert^{8}))\,.

Proof.

We observe

\displaystyle\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},(v+u)^{\otimes 4}\rangle+\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},(v-u)^{\otimes 4}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad=2\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},v^{\otimes 4}\rangle+2\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},u^{\otimes 4}\rangle+12\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle\,.

Then, using
Fact

3.8
,

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u,v}$}}}$}}}

\displaystyle\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq O\Bigg(\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},(v+u)^{\otimes 4}\rangle^{2}+\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},(v-u)^{\otimes 4}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},v^{\otimes 4}\rangle^{2}+\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},u^{\otimes 4}\rangle^{2}\Bigg)\,.

We can now apply the assumption to obtain

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u,v}$}}}$}}}

\displaystyle\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle^{2}\leq O\left(\varepsilon\lVert v+u\rVert^{8}+\varepsilon\lVert v-u\rVert^{8}+\varepsilon\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\varepsilon\lVert u\rVert^{8}\right)

\displaystyle\leq O(\varepsilon)(\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\lVert u\rVert^{8})\,.

∎

In
Lemma

5.17
we prove that we can construct a system of constraints
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v)
that is roughly equivalent to
{\mathcal{A}}(v)
.

Lemma 5.17

(Approximate moment feasibility)
.

Suppose the mixture is in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position.
Given
\mathrm{poly}(d,w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1})
samples from the mixture with an
\varepsilon
-fraction of corruptions, where
\varepsilon\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{4})
, there exists an algorithm that runs in time
\mathrm{poly}(d,w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1})
and computes a system of polynomial inequalities
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)
of size
\mathrm{poly}(d)
in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that with high probability

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}(v,O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/4}))

and

{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/4}))\,.

Proof.

We compute by
Corollary

5.15
in time
\mathrm{poly}(d,w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1})
some
\widehat{M}_{4}
such that

\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},v^{\otimes 4}\rangle^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-2}\sqrt{\varepsilon})\lVert v\rVert^{8}\,.

Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}=O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/4})
. Then we construct the following system of polynomial inequalities
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon^{\prime})
in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

1.

\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
,

2.

\left(\langle\widehat{M}_{4}/3,v^{\otimes 4}\rangle-\lVert v\rVert^{4}\right)^{2}\leq(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{8}
,

3.

there exists a degree-
O(1)
sum-of-squares proof in indeterminate
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{u}$}}}$}}}\left\{\left(4\langle\widehat{M}_{4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle-4\lVert v\rVert^{2}\lVert u\rVert^{2}-8\langle v,u\rangle^{2}\right)^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\left(\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\lVert u\rVert^{8}\right)\right\}\,.

We start by proving that
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}(v,O(\varepsilon^{\prime}))
. We have trivially that
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
. For the second constraint, we have by
Fact

3.8

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left(\langle M_{4}/3,v^{\otimes 4}\rangle-\lVert v\rVert^{4}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\langle\widehat{M}_{4}/3,v^{\otimes 4}\rangle-\lVert v\rVert^{4}\right)^{2}+2\langle M_{4}/3-\widehat{M}_{4}/3,v^{\otimes 4}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O((\varepsilon^{\prime})^{2})\lVert v\rVert^{8}\,.

For the third constraint, we have by
Fact

3.8
and
Lemma

5.16

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v,u}$}}}$}}}

\displaystyle\left(4\langle M_{4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle-4\lVert v\rVert^{2}\lVert u\rVert^{2}-8\langle v,u\rangle^{2}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(4\langle\widehat{M}_{4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle-4\lVert v\rVert^{2}\lVert u\rVert^{2}-8\langle v,u\rangle^{2}\right)^{2}+2\left(4\langle M_{4}-\widehat{M}_{4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}\left(\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\lVert u\rVert^{8}\right)\right)\,.

Therefore
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}(v,O(\varepsilon^{\prime}))
.

A similar set of inequalities gives the other direction.
∎

Finally, we show that even if the mixture is only in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position,
{\mathcal{A}}(v)
is still roughly equivalent to the system of constraints that we would have if the mixture were in exact isotropic position.

Lemma 5.18

(Approximate isotropic position)
.

Suppose the mixture is in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position, with
\gamma\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{2})
.
Let
\tilde{\Sigma}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\Sigma_{i}
, and let
\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)
be the system of polynomial inequalities in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in
Definition

5.9

for the mixture

\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{x}
where
\bm{x}
is distributed according to the ground truth mixture, that is, for the ground truth mixture put into exact isotropic position.
Then

{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,O(\varepsilon+w_{\min}^{-2}\gamma))

and

\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}(v,O(\varepsilon+w_{\min}^{-2}\gamma))\,.

Proof.

We start by proving that
{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,O(\varepsilon+w_{\min}^{-2}\gamma))
.
We have trivially that
{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
.

For the second constraint,
we have by
Fact

3.8
and
Fact

3.7

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}/3-\lVert v\rVert^{4}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}/3-\lVert v\rVert^{4}\right)^{2}+2\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}/3-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}/3\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{8}+2\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}/3-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}/3\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=2\varepsilon^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{8}+2\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}(\langle\bm{x},v\rangle+\langle(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d})\bm{x},v\rangle)^{4}/3-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}/3\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{8}+O\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{3}\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{\ell}\langle(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d})\bm{x},v\rangle^{4-\ell}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{8}+O\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{3}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2\ell}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d})\bm{x},v\rangle^{8-2\ell}\right)\,,

where the last line uses
Fact

3.15
.
Because of the
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position we have
\lVert\Sigma_{i}\rVert\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1})
for all
i\in[k]
, so we can bound for any constant
t
using
Fact

3.13

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2t}\leq O\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v)^{t}\right)\leq O(w_{\min}^{-t})\lVert v\rVert^{2t}\,.

Similarly, using that
\lVert(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d})\Sigma_{i}(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d})\rVert\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1}\gamma^{2})
,

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d})\bm{x},v\rangle^{2t}\leq O\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}(v^{\top}(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d})^{\top}\Sigma_{i}(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d})v)^{t}\right)\leq O(w_{\min}^{-t}\gamma^{2t})\lVert v\rVert^{2t}\,.

Therefore we get

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}/3-\lVert v\rVert^{4}\right)^{2}\leq 2\varepsilon^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{8}+O(w_{\min}^{-4}\gamma^{2})\lVert v\rVert^{8}\,.

For the third constraint, we have by
Fact

3.8

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{3.32222pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v,u}$}}}$}}}\left(4\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{x})^{\otimes 4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle-4\lVert v\rVert^{2}\lVert u\rVert^{2}-8\langle v,u\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(4\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\bm{x}^{\otimes 4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle-4\lVert v\rVert^{2}\lVert u\rVert^{2}-8\langle v,u\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2\left(4\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{x})^{\otimes 4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle-4\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\bm{x}^{\otimes 4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon(\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\lVert u\rVert^{8})+2\left(4\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{x})^{\otimes 4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle-4\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\bm{x}^{\otimes 4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle\right)^{2}\,.

We will make use of
Lemma

5.16
, so it will suffice to bound the second term above for the case
v=u
.
We have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}2\left(4\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{x})^{\otimes 4},v^{\otimes 4}\rangle-4\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\bm{x}^{\otimes 4},v^{\otimes 4}\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}/3-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{4}\rangle/3\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O\left(w_{\min}^{-4}\gamma^{2}\right)\lVert v\rVert^{8}

where the last inequality follows by the bound we proved for the second constraint. Then by
Lemma

5.16
an analogous bound also applies to the term with
v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}
. Therefore we get

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left(4\langle\mathop{\mathbb{E}}(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{x})^{\otimes 4},v^{\otimes 2}\otimes u^{\otimes 2}\rangle-2\lVert v\rVert^{2}\lVert u\rVert^{2}-4\langle v,u\rangle\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon(\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\lVert u\rVert^{8})+O(w_{\min}^{-4}\gamma^{2})(\lVert v\rVert^{8}+\lVert u\rVert^{8})\,.

Hence, from the bounds we got for the two constraints, we get that
{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,O(\varepsilon+w_{\min}^{-2}\gamma))
.

A similar set of inequalities gives the other direction.
∎

5.1.3
Final Constraint Construction

In this section, we show that we can construct
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
that satisfies the conditions of the rounding algorithm analyzed in
Theorem

4.2
.

Lemma 5.19

(Centered mixtures rounding conditions)
.

Suppose the mixture is in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position.
Let
{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)
be the system of polynomial inequalities for the mixture.
Let
\tilde{\Sigma}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\Sigma_{i}
, and let
\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)
be the system of polynomial inequalities in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in
Definition

5.9

for the mixture

\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\bm{x}
where
\bm{x}
is distributed according to the ground truth mixture, that is, for the ground truth mixture put into exact isotropic position.

Let
P_{i}
be the orthogonal projection to the subspace of eigenvectors of
\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}
such that their eigenvalues lie outside
[1-\delta,1+\delta]
.
Also let
\bar{P}_{i}
be the orthogonal projection to the subspace of eigenvectors of
\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}
such that their eigenvalues lie outside
[1-\delta^{1/2048},1+\delta^{1/2048}]
.
Finally, let
R\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
be the orthogonal projection to the span of the subspaces associated with
P_{i}
for all
i\in[k]
.

Suppose
\varepsilon\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{16})
,
\gamma\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{6}\varepsilon)
, and
\delta\sim w_{\min}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}
small enough.
Given
\mathrm{poly}(d,w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1})
samples from the mixture with an
\varepsilon
-fraction of corruptions, there exists an algorithm that runs in time
\mathrm{poly}(d,w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1})
and computes a system of polynomial inequalities
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
of size
\mathrm{poly}(d)
in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, including
\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
, such that with high probability:

1.

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\{\left\lVert\bar{P}_{i}v\right\rVert^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-4}\varepsilon^{1/128})\right\}
for all
i\in[k]
,

2.

\left\{\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1,Rv=0\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

5.17
and
Lemma

5.18
we can construct a system of polynomial inequalities
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)
in time
\mathrm{poly}(d,w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1})
such that

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}(v,O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/4}))

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(v,O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/4}+w_{\min}^{-2}\gamma)\,.

Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}=O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/4}+w_{\min}^{-2}\gamma)
.
Then by
Lemma

5.12
, for all
i\in[k]
,

\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\{\|\bar{P}_{i}v\|^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-3}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{1/16}\delta^{-1/1024})\}\,,

where using that
\delta^{1/1024}\geq\Omega((\varepsilon^{\prime})^{1/32})
we have the bound

\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\{\|\bar{P}_{i}v\|^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-3}(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{1/32})\}\,,

where using the bounds on
\varepsilon
and
\gamma
we get the bound

\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\{\|\bar{P}_{i}v\|^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-4}\varepsilon^{1/128})\}\,.

For the second claim, we start by noting that, by
Lemma

5.13
, there exists a sum-of-squares proof in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
that, if
\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
and
Rv=0
, then
v
is feasible for
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(v,O(k^{2}\delta^{2}))
.
Then by
Lemma

5.17
and
Lemma

5.18

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(v,\Theta(k^{2}\delta^{2}))

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}(v,O(k^{2}\delta^{2}+w_{\min}^{-2}\gamma))

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\hat{\mathcal{A}}\left(v,O(w_{\min}^{-1}(k^{2}\delta^{2}+w_{\min}^{-2}\gamma)^{1/4})\right)\,.

When
\gamma\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{6}\varepsilon)
and
\delta\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{3}\varepsilon^{2})
, this implies
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)
.
∎

We also prove that the subspace we aim to find has dimension bounded by
{\max_{i}\lVert\Sigma_{i}-I_{d}\rVert_{F}^{2}}
.

Lemma 5.20

(Rank bound)
.

Let
A_{1},\ldots,A_{k}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
.
For all
i\in[k]
, let
P_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
be the orthogonal projection to the subspace of eigenvectors of
A_{i}
such that their eigenvalues lie outside
[1-\delta,1+\delta]
.
Let
P\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
be the orthogonal projection to the span of the subspaces associated with
P_{i}
for all
i\in[k]
.
Suppose that
\|A_{i}-I_{d}\|_{F}^{2}\leq r
for all
i\in[k]
. Then
\mathrm{rank}(P)\leq kr/\delta^{2}
.

Proof.

First, we note that
\mathrm{rank}(P)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\mathrm{rank}(P_{i})
and thus it suffices to bound each
\mathrm{rank}(P_{i})
. For some fixed
i\in[k]
, let
\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{d}
be the eigenvalues of
A_{i}-I_{d}
. We have that

\displaystyle r\geq\|A_{i}-I_{d}\|_{F}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lambda_{j}^{2}\geq\delta^{2}\mathrm{rank}(P_{i})\,.

Then
\mathrm{rank}(P_{i})\leq r/\delta^{2}
for all
i\in[k]
and therefore
\mathrm{rank}(P)\leq kr/\delta^{2}
.
∎

5.1.4
