Proof of
Theorem

5.6

Let
\tilde{\Sigma}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\Sigma_{i}
.
Let
P_{i}
be the orthogonal projection to the subspace of eigenvectors of
\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}
such that their eigenvalues lie outside
[1-\delta,1+\delta]
, where
\delta\sim w_{\min}^{3}\varepsilon^{2}
.
Also let
\bar{P}_{i}
be the orthogonal projection to the subspace of eigenvectors of
\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}
such that their eigenvalues lie outside
[1-\delta^{1/2048},1+\delta^{1/2048}]
.
Finally, let
R\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
be the orthogonal projection to the span of the subspaces associated with
P_{i}
for all
i\in[k]
.

By
Lemma

5.19
, we can compute in time
\mathrm{poly}(d,w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1})
a system of polynomial inequalities
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
of size
\mathrm{poly}(d)
in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, including
\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
, such that

•

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\{\left\lVert\bar{P}_{i}v\right\rVert^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-4}\varepsilon^{1/128})\right\}
for all
i\in[k]
,

•

\left\{\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1,Rv=0\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
.

Furthermore, we have for all
i\in[k]
that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\tilde{\Sigma}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\left\lVert\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert\Sigma_{i}-\tilde{\Sigma}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\left\lVert\Sigma_{i}-\tilde{\Sigma}\right\rVert_{F}\right)\leq O\left(\left\lVert\Sigma_{i}-I_{d}\right\rVert_{F}+\left\lVert\tilde{\Sigma}-I_{d}\right\rVert_{F}\right)\leq O(\sqrt{r}+\gamma)\,.

Then, by
Lemma

5.20
,
\operatorname{rank}(R)\leq O(k(r+\gamma^{2})/\delta^{2})\leq O(w_{\min}^{-8}\varepsilon^{-4}r)
.

Then, by applying
Theorem

4.2
to
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
with orthogonal projection matrices
\bar{P}_{1},\ldots,\bar{P}_{k}
, and
R
, we can compute in time
f(w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1},r)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(d)
a
D
-dimensional subspace such that, for every unit vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\lVert\bar{P}_{i}v\rVert^{2}\geq 1-\alpha
for some
i\in[k]
, there exists a unit vector in the subspace that is
\beta
-close to it, where

D=O(w_{\min}^{1/2}\varepsilon^{-1/1024})^{O(w_{\min}^{-8}\varepsilon^{-4}r)}\,,

\alpha=\Omega(w_{\min}^{-4}\varepsilon^{1/128})\,,

\beta=O(w_{\min}^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{1/1024})\,.

We argue now that the unit vectors
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
\lVert\bar{P}_{i}v\rVert^{2}\geq 1-\alpha
are the ones we are interested in.
First, we verify that every unit vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
v^{\top}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}v\leq\Omega(\alpha)
for some
i\in[k]
also satisfies
\lVert\bar{P}_{i}v\rVert^{2}\geq 1-\alpha
.
By the definition of
\bar{P}_{i}
, we have that
\lVert(I_{d}-\bar{P}_{i})v\rVert^{2}\leq O(v^{\top}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}v)
, so if
v^{\top}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}v\leq\Omega(\alpha)
then
\lVert(I_{d}-\bar{P}_{i})v\rVert^{2}\leq\Omega(\alpha)
so
\lVert\bar{P}_{i}v\rVert^{2}\geq 1-\Omega(\alpha)\geq 1-\alpha
.
Second, we want a result about
v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v
, not
v^{\top}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}v
, but we note that

\displaystyle|v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v-v^{\top}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}v|

\displaystyle=|v^{\top}(\Sigma_{i}-\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2})v|\leq\lVert v\rVert^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{i}-\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\rVert

\displaystyle=\lVert\Sigma_{i}-(I_{d}+E)\Sigma_{i}(I_{d}+E)\rVert\leq 2\lVert E\rVert\lVert\Sigma_{i}\rVert+\lVert E\rVert^{2}\lVert\Sigma_{i}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1}\gamma)\,,

where we used that
E=\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d}
satisfies
\lVert E\rVert\leq O(\gamma)
and
\lVert\Sigma_{i}\rVert\leq w_{\min}^{-1}
.
Therefore, because
w_{\min}^{-1}\gamma\ll\alpha
, any unit vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v\leq\Omega(\alpha)
also satisfies
v^{\top}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma_{i}\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}v\leq\Omega(\alpha)
.
This finishes the proof.

∎

5.2
Algorithm for Clustering Mixtures of Centered Gaussians

We first present the algorithm and its main subroutines and then conclude by proving
Theorem

5.2
.
Our algorithm has two main subroutines:

1.

Partial clustering refinement:
This subroutine takes a partial clustering
\mathcal{S}
and produces a list of candidate refinements with the following properties:

(a)

Every partial clustering
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
in the list is a refinement of
\mathcal{S}
,

(b)

The size of the output list is bounded by
f(w_{\min}^{-1},\Delta)
for some function
f
,

(c)

If
\mathcal{S}
is “good”, then with high probability the output list contains a partial clustering that is good.

2.

Clustering selection:
This subroutine takes a list of candidate clusterings that contains at least one “good” clustering and outputs a good clustering.

Algorithm 2

Mixtures of centered Gaussians learning algorithm

input :
a collection of samples
\mathcal{T}
, the number of components
k
, the minimum mixing weight
w_{\min}
, the separation parameter
\Delta

output :
a clustering
\mathcal{S}
of
\mathcal{T}

1

Partition
\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{T}_{1}\cup\mathcal{T}_{2}
by putting each sample in
\mathcal{T}_{1}
independently with probability
1/2
\the\algocf@printnl@i{{{3}}}footnote
\the\algocf@printnl@i{{{3}}}footnote
\algocf@printnl@i{{{3}}}footnote:

\the\algocf@printnl@i{{{3}}}footnote

4

This increases the fraction of outliers in
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}
with high probability by at most a constant factor.
;

2

Let
\mathcal{C}=\left\{\mathcal{S}\right\}
where
\mathcal{S}
is the trivial partial clustering with one subset
\mathcal{T}_{1}
;

3

while

\exists\,\mathcal{S}\in\mathcal{C}
such that
|\mathcal{S}|<k

do

4

Remove
\mathcal{S}
from
\mathcal{C}
;

5

Let
L
be the output of the partial clustering refinement algorithm (
Algorithm

3
) on
\mathcal{S}
;

6

Add all
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\in L
to
\mathcal{C}
;

7

end while

8

Run the clustering selection algorithm (
Lemma

A.1
) on
\mathcal{C}
with upper bound
\Delta^{-1/k^{(4k^{2})}}
on the fraction of corruptions and with new samples
\mathcal{T}_{2}
, and let
\mathcal{S}
be the returned clustering;

9

return

\mathcal{S}
;

Algorithm 3

Mixtures of centered Gaussians partial clustering refinement algorithm

input :
a candidate partial clustering
\mathcal{S}
, the number of components
k
, the minimum mixing weight
w_{\min}
, the separation parameter
\Delta

output :
a list of candidate refinements of
\mathcal{S}

1

Let
L=\varnothing
;

2

for

each
S\in\mathcal{S}
and each guess of
\varepsilon
with
2^{-\Delta}
-bit complexity that satisfies
\varepsilon\geq\Delta^{-1}

do

//
Frobenius clustering

3

for

2^{O(w_{\min}^{-1}\log(k/\varepsilon^{1/4}))}
rounds

do

4

Let
S=S_{1}\cup S_{2}
be the result of the Frobenius partial clustering algorithm (
Fact

3.22
) on
S
with parameters
\varepsilon
,
\alpha=w_{\min}
,
t=4
, and
\beta=\varepsilon
;

5

Add
(\mathcal{S}\setminus S)\cup\{S_{1},S_{2}\}
to
L

6

end for

7

Put the samples of
S
in isotropic position with the algorithm corresponding to
Fact

3.21
;

//
Spectral clustering

8

Let
Q
be the subspace returned by the zero-mean subspace recovery algorithm (
Theorem

5.6
) run on
S
with fraction of outliers set to
\varepsilon^{1/12}
(but abort and set
Q=\emptyset
if the algorithm performs more than
f(\Delta)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(d)
steps for some function
f
);

9

If
\dim(Q)>f(\Delta)
for some function
f
, set
Q=\emptyset
;

10

for

each unit vector
v
in a
\Delta^{-1}
-net of
Q

do

11

for

each
\varepsilon
-resolution choice of
\tau
in
\left[\varepsilon,2w_{\min}^{-1}\right]

do

12

Partition the samples
S=S_{1}\cup S_{2}
based on whether their projection on
v
is in the interval
[-\tau,\tau]
or outside it;

13

Add
(\mathcal{S}\setminus S)\cup\{S_{1},S_{2}\}
to
L
;

14

end for

15

end for

16

end for

17

return

L
;

We first state a few facts that we use in the analysis of the algorithm.

Fact 5.21

(Lemma 5.1 in
[
19
]
)
.

Consider two arbitrary positive definite matrices
\Sigma_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
\Sigma_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, and suppose there exists a positive definite matrix
H\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
such that

\left\lVert I_{d}-H^{-1/2}\Sigma_{1}H^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\rho,\quad\left\lVert I_{d}-H^{-1/2}\Sigma_{2}H^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\rho\,.

Then, for an arbitrary positive definite matrix
\Sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, we have

\left\lVert\Sigma^{-1/2}\Sigma_{1}\Sigma^{-1/2}-\Sigma^{-1/2}\Sigma_{2}\Sigma^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{F}\leq 5\rho\max\left(\left\lVert\Sigma^{-1/2}\Sigma_{1}\Sigma^{-1/2}\right\rVert,\left\lVert\Sigma^{-1/2}\Sigma_{2}\Sigma^{-1/2}\right\rVert\right)\,.

Claim 5.22

(Quadratic form with approximate vectors)
.

Let
\Sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
be a matrix with
\lVert\Sigma\rVert\leq\Delta
. Suppose
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a unit vector and
w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is a vector such that
\lVert v-w\rVert\leq\delta\leq 1
. Then
{|v^{\top}\Sigma v-w^{\top}\Sigma w|\leq O(\delta\Delta)}
.

Proof.

We have

\displaystyle w^{\top}\Sigma w

\displaystyle=(v+(w-v))^{\top}\Sigma(v+(w-v))\leq v^{\top}\Sigma v+2\lVert w-v\rVert\lVert\Sigma\rVert+\lVert w-v\rVert^{2}\lVert\Sigma\rVert\leq v^{\top}\Sigma v+O(\delta\Delta)

and

\displaystyle w^{\top}\Sigma w

\displaystyle=(v+(w-v))^{\top}\Sigma(v+(w-v))\geq v^{\top}\Sigma v-2\lVert w-v\rVert\lVert\Sigma\rVert-\lVert w-v\rVert^{2}\lVert\Sigma\rVert\geq v^{\top}\Sigma v-O(\delta\Delta)\,.

∎

Claim 5.23

(Variance thresholding)
.

Consider two arbitrary positive definite matrices
\Sigma_{1}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
\Sigma_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
.
Let
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and suppose
v^{\top}\Sigma_{1}v<v^{\top}\Sigma_{2}v
. Denote
{\sigma_{1}=\sqrt{v^{\top}\Sigma_{1}v}}
and
{\sigma_{2}=\sqrt{v^{\top}\Sigma_{2}v}}
. Then

\mathbb{P}_{\bm{x}\sim N(0,\Sigma_{1})}\left(\langle\bm{x},v\rangle\not\in[-\sqrt{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}},\sqrt{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}]\right)\leq\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}

and

\mathbb{P}_{\bm{x}\sim N(0,\Sigma_{2})}\left(\langle\bm{x},v\rangle\in[-\sqrt{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}},\sqrt{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}]\right)\leq O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}}\right)\,.

Proof.

We use that, for an arbitrary positive definite matrix
\Sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
\bm{x}\sim N(0,\Sigma)
,
\langle\bm{x},v\rangle
is distributed according to
N(0,v^{\top}\Sigma v)
.
Then, for the first inequality, we have by Chebyshev’s inequality

\mathbb{P}_{\bm{x}\sim N(0,\Sigma_{1})}\left(|\langle\bm{x},v\rangle|\geq\sigma_{1}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma_{1}}}\right)\leq\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}\,.

For the second inequality, we have by the anti-concentration properties of Gaussians that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\bm{x}\sim N(0,\Sigma_{2})}\left(|\langle\bm{x},v\rangle|\leq\sigma_{2}\cdot\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq O\left(\sqrt{\frac{\sigma_{1}}{\sigma_{2}}}\right)\,.

∎

5.2.1
Analysis of Partial Clustering Refinement (
Algorithm

3
)

We now show that the refinement subroutine has the desired properties.
First, we show that the subroutine produces a good partial clustering in the case of relative Frobenius separation.

Lemma 5.24

(Frobenius clustering result)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a
(1-\varepsilon)
-good partial clustering.
Suppose there exists some
S\in\mathcal{S}
with
|S|\geq\mathrm{poly}(d,k^{k},\Delta)
that contains at least two components
i,j\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
such that
\left\lVert\Sigma_{S}^{-1/2}(\Sigma_{i}-\Sigma_{j})\Sigma_{S}^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{F}\geq\varepsilon^{-1}
, where
\Sigma_{S}=\sum_{i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)}w^{\prime}_{i}\Sigma_{i}
for
w^{\prime}_{i}=\frac{w_{i}}{\sum_{i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)}w_{i}}
.
Then, for
\Delta^{-1}\leq\varepsilon\leq w_{\min}^{O(1)}
, the output of
Algorithm

3
contains with high probability some
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
that is a
(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good
partial clustering for
\varepsilon^{\prime}=O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/4})
.

Proof.

Consider the iteration of the loop in which we select a cluster
S
and a value
\varepsilon
that correspond to the assumptions.
We show that with high probability in this case one of the iterations of the Frobenius clustering inner loop will produce a
(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good refinement of
\mathcal{S}
.

By the assumptions, there exist some
i,j\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
such that
\left\lVert\Sigma_{S}^{-1/2}(\Sigma_{i}-\Sigma_{j})\Sigma_{S}^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{F}\geq\varepsilon^{-1}
.
Then to run the partial clustering algorithm in
Fact

3.22
with
\varepsilon
,
\alpha=w_{\min}
,
t=1
, and
\beta=\varepsilon^{1/4}
,
we require that
\left\lVert\Sigma_{S}^{-1/2}(\Sigma_{i}-\Sigma_{j})\Sigma_{S}^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\geq\Omega(k^{2}/(\beta^{2}\alpha^{4}))=\Omega(k^{2}w_{\min}^{-4}\varepsilon^{-1/2})
, which is smaller than
\varepsilon^{-1}
for our choice of parameters.
Therefore the partial clustering algorithm succeeds with probability
2^{-O\left(w_{\min}^{-1}\log(k/\varepsilon^{1/4})\right)}
.

If the partial clustering algorithm succeeds, then it partitions the samples into two while making an error on at most an
O\left(\beta+\varepsilon/\alpha^{4}\right)
-fraction of the samples from each component, which is dominated by
O(\beta)
.
Therefore in each part the fraction of outliers increases by at most
O(w_{\min}^{-1}\beta)=O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/4})
, and the new clustering is
(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good for
\varepsilon^{\prime}=O(\varepsilon^{1/4})
.
Furthermore, since we run the algorithm
2^{O\left(w_{\min}^{-1}\log(k/\varepsilon^{1/4})\right)}
times, with high probability at least one of these runs succeeds and the corresponding iteration adds a
(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good refinement to the output list.

Finally, we note that we do not need the exact value of
\varepsilon
and an upper bound within
2^{-\Delta}
of the truth suffices.
∎

Second, we give a similar result in the case of spectral separation.

Lemma 5.25

(Spectral clustering result)
.

Let
\mathcal{S}
be a
(1-\varepsilon)
-good partial clustering.
Suppose there exists some
S\in\mathcal{S}
with the following properties:

1.

|S|\geq\mathrm{poly}(d,\Delta)
,

2.

S
contains at least two components
i,j\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
such that there exists some unit vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v>\Delta^{1/2}\cdot v^{\top}\Sigma_{j}v
,

3.

The mixture corresponding to components in
\mathsf{comp}(S)
is in approximate isotropic position:
(1-\varepsilon^{3})I_{d}\preceq\sum_{i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)}w^{\prime}_{i}\Sigma_{i}\preceq(1+\varepsilon^{3})I_{d}
and
\left\lVert\sum_{i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)}w^{\prime}_{i}\Sigma_{i}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon^{3}
, where
w^{\prime}_{i}=\frac{w_{i}}{\sum_{i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)}w_{i}}
,

4.

For all
i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
, we have that
\lVert\Sigma_{i}-I_{d}\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq f(\Delta)
for some function
f
.

Then, for
\Delta^{-1}\leq\varepsilon\leq w_{\min}^{O(k)}
, the output of
Algorithm

3
contains with high probability some
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
that is a
(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good partial clustering for
\varepsilon^{\prime}=O(w_{\min}^{-2}\varepsilon^{1/(4096k)})
.

Proof.

Consider the iteration of the loop in which we select a cluster
S
and a value
\varepsilon
that correspond to the assumptions.
We show that with high probability in this case the inner loop will produce a
(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good refinement of
\mathcal{S}
.

When we run the subspace recovery algorithm in
Theorem

5.6
, we obtain with high probability a subspace such that, for each unit
v
for which
v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{-4}\varepsilon^{1/128})
for some
i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
, there exists a unit vector in the subspace that is
O(w_{\min}^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{1/1024})
-close to it.

By the assumptions, we have that for some
i,j\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
there exists a unit vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
with
v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v>\Delta^{1/2}\cdot v^{\top}\Sigma_{j}v
.
By the approximate isotropic position, we can assume that
{v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v\leq 2w_{\min}^{-1}}
, so
v^{\top}\Sigma_{j}v\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1}\Delta^{-1/2})
.
Then the guarantee from
Theorem

5.6
shows that there exists a unit vector in the recovered subspace that is
O(w_{\min}^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{1/1024})
-close to
v
as long as
{v^{\top}\Sigma_{j}v\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{-4}\varepsilon^{1/128})}
, which we satisfy because
v^{\top}\Sigma_{j}v\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1}\Delta^{-1/2})\ll w_{\min}^{-4}\varepsilon^{1/128}
.
Then there also exists a unit vector
v^{\prime}
in the
\Delta^{-1}
-net
of the subspace that is
O(w_{\min}^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{1/1024}+\Delta^{-1})
-close to
v
, which is dominated by
O(w_{\min}^{-1/2}\varepsilon^{1/1024})
.

Then, by
Claim

5.22
and using that
\lVert\Sigma_{j}\rVert\leq 2w_{\min}^{-1}
, we have that
v^{\prime}
satisfies
(v^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{j}(v^{\prime})\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1}\Delta^{-1/2})+O(w_{\min}^{-3/2}\varepsilon^{1/1024})
, which is dominated by
O(w_{\min}^{-3/2}\varepsilon^{1/1024})
.
Then, using that because of the approximate isotropic position
\max_{i}(v^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{i}(v^{\prime})\geq 1/2
, if we sort all components in increasing order by their variance in direction
v^{\prime}
, there exist two consecutive indices
i
and
i+1
such that
(v^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{i+1}v^{\prime}\geq\Omega(w_{\min}^{-3/2}\varepsilon^{1/1024})
and
(v^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v^{\prime}/{(v^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{i+1}v^{\prime}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-3/(2k)}\varepsilon^{1/(1024k)})}
. Consider partitioning the samples projected along direction
v^{\prime}
with the threshold
\tau=\sqrt{(v^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v^{\prime}\cdot(v^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{i+1}v^{\prime}}
. Then by
Claim

5.23
an independent sample from the mixture is partitioned erroneously with probability at most
O(w_{\min}^{-3/(8k)}\varepsilon^{1/(4096k)})
.

Then, by Hoeffding’s inequality, we have that with probability
\exp\left(-|S|\cdot O(w_{\min}^{-3/(4k)}\varepsilon^{1/(2048k)})\right)
at most an
O(w_{\min}^{-3/(8k)}\varepsilon^{1/(4096k)})
-fraction of the samples are partitioned erroneously.
In the worst case in which one part contains only one component with weight
w_{\min}
, the fraction of outliers in it is at most
O(w_{\min}^{-2}\varepsilon^{1/(4096k)})
.

A problem we omitted is that the bound on the probability that a sample is partitioned erroneously holds for a fixed direction independent of the samples, but
v^{\prime}
can depend on the samples.
We can show however by a union bound that the result holds for all
v^{\prime}
.
Consider a
\mathrm{poly}(d^{-1},\Delta^{-1})
-net of
\mathbb{R}^{d}
, which has size
\mathrm{poly}(d,\Delta)^{d}
.
The probability that a sample is partitioned erroneously in a direction that is
\mathrm{poly}(d^{-1},\Delta^{-1})
-close to
v^{\prime}
is the same up to constants to the probability for direction
v^{\prime}
, so the bound by Hoeffding’s inequality is also essentially the same.
Then to union bound over all the directions in the net it suffices to have
|S|\geq\mathrm{poly}(d,\Delta)
.

It may also happen that
(v^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v^{\prime}
is
much
smaller than
(v^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{i+1}v^{\prime}
, and that the threshold
\tau=\sqrt{(v^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v^{\prime}\cdot(v^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{i+1}v^{\prime}}
falls below the interval in which we search for it. However, we obtain the same guarantees also with the threshold
\tau=\sqrt{\max\left\{(v^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{i}v^{\prime},\varepsilon\right\}\cdot(v^{\prime})^{\top}\Sigma_{i+1}v^{\prime}}
, so this is not an issue.

Furthermore, we note that we obtain essentially the same probability of partitioning a sample erroneously even if the threshold is within a constant multiplicative factor from the threshold
\tau
we analyzed.
Also note that the threshold is trivially upper bounded by
2w_{\min}^{-1}
, because
\lVert\Sigma_{i}\rVert\leq 2w_{\min}^{-1}
for all
i\in[k]
.

Thus, when we loop over every unit vector in the net, we find a direction
v^{\prime}
for which we produce a
(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good partial clustering for
\varepsilon^{\prime}=O(w_{\min}^{-2}\varepsilon^{1/(4096k)})
.

Finally, we note that we do not need the exact value of
\varepsilon
and an upper bound within
2^{-\Delta}
of the truth suffices.
∎

We also show that the refinement procedure produces a list of dimension-independent size.

Lemma 5.26

(Partial clustering refinement produces a small list)
.

For all partitions
\mathcal{S}
of the sample universe such that
|\mathcal{S}|<k
, the size of the list returned by
Algorithm

3
has size at most
f(w_{\min}^{-1},\Delta)
for some function
f
.

Proof.

We first consider the number of candidate refinements added to the list in a single iteration of the loop.
In the first part of the loop, we run the Frobenius clustering algorithm
2^{O(w_{\min}^{-1}\log(k/\varepsilon))}
times and for each run of the algorithm we add one possible refinement to the output list, where
\varepsilon\geq\Delta^{-1}
.
In the second part of the loop, we run the centered mixture subspace recovery algorithm and for each
v
in a
\Delta^{-1}
-net of the outputted subspace we add at most
f(w_{\min}^{-1},\Delta)
possible refinements to the output list for some function
f
.
The dimension of the subspace outputted by the centered mixture subspace recovery algorithm in
Theorem

5.6
is at most some function of
\Delta
.
Then, the size of the
\Delta^{-1}
-net of the subspace is also bounded by some function of
\Delta
.
Finally, the number of iterations of the loop is bounded by a function exponential in
\Delta
.
∎

Finally, we combine the previous lemmas to give the overall guarantees of the refinement procedure.

Lemma 5.27

(Correctness of partial clustering refinement)
.

Given a partial clustering
\mathcal{S}
with
|\mathcal{S}|<k
,
Algorithm

3
outputs a list of partial clusterings
L
with the following properties:

1.

Every
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}\in L
is a refinement of
\mathcal{S}
,

2.

The size of
L
is bounded by
f(w_{\min}^{-1},\Delta)
for some function
f
,

3.

If
\mathcal{S}
is a
(1-\varepsilon)
-good partial clustering for
\Delta^{-1}\leq\varepsilon\leq w_{\min}^{(2k^{2k})}
with
|S|\geq\mathrm{poly}(d,k^{k},\Delta)
for all
S\in\mathcal{S}
, then the output of the algorithm contains with high probability at least one refinement
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
that is a
(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good clustering with
\varepsilon^{\prime}=O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/(4k^{2k})})
.

Proof.

The first property is immediate from the definition of the algorithm. The second property follows by
Lemma

5.26
.

For the rest of the proof, suppose that
\mathcal{S}
is a
(1-\varepsilon)
-good partial clustering with
|S|\geq\mathrm{poly}(d,k^{k},\Delta)
for all
S\in\mathcal{S}
.
Because the size of
\mathcal{S}
is less than
k
, for some cluster
S\in\mathcal{S}
we have
|\mathsf{comp}(S)|>1
.
For such a cluster with more than one component, let
{\Sigma_{S}=\sum_{i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)}w^{\prime}_{i}\Sigma_{i}}
for
{w^{\prime}_{i}=\frac{w_{i}}{\sum_{i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)}w_{i}}}
.
If there exist two
i,j\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
such that
\left\lVert\Sigma_{S}^{-1/2}(\Sigma_{i}-\Sigma_{j})\Sigma_{S}^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{F}\geq\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}
, then we satisfy the conditions of
Lemma

5.24
and with high probability the Frobenius clustering produces a
(1-O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/(4k^{2k})}))
-good clustering.

Else,
\left\lVert\Sigma_{S}^{-1/2}(\Sigma_{i}-\Sigma_{j})\Sigma_{S}^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}
for all
i,j\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
, so the robust isotropic position algorithm corresponding to
Fact

3.21
succeeds with high probability and we can assume the mixture is in approximate isotropic position, with new mixture mean and covariance
{\left\lVert\mu^{\prime}\right\rVert\leq\varepsilon^{1/4}}
,
{(1-\varepsilon^{1/4})I_{d}\preceq\Sigma^{\prime}\preceq(1+\varepsilon^{1/4})I_{d}}
, and
{\left\lVert\Sigma^{\prime}-I_{d}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon^{1/4}}
.
Denote by
\Sigma_{1}^{\prime},\ldots,\Sigma_{k}^{\prime}
the component covariances after this robust isotropic position transformation.
Then we also have that
{\left\lVert(\Sigma^{\prime})^{-1/2}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}-\Sigma_{j}^{\prime})(\Sigma^{\prime})^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}}
for all
i,j\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
, so

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}-\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert(\Sigma^{\prime})^{1/2}(\Sigma^{\prime})^{-1/2}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}-\Sigma_{j}^{\prime})(\Sigma^{\prime})^{-1/2}(\Sigma^{\prime})^{1/2}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert(\Sigma^{\prime})^{1/2}\right\rVert^{2}\cdot\left\lVert(\Sigma^{\prime})^{-1/2}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}-\Sigma_{j}^{\prime})(\Sigma^{\prime})^{-1/2}\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}\,.

Note that the approximate isotropic position implies that
\left\lVert\sum_{i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)}w^{\prime}_{i}\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}-I_{d}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon^{1/4}
, which is false unless
\left\lVert\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}-I_{d}\right\rVert_{F}\leq 4\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}
for all
i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
. This is because, using that
\left\lVert\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}-\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}\right\rVert_{F}\leq 2\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}
for all
i,j\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
, we get that for any fixed
i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
all
\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}
with
j\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
are
2\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}
-close to
\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}
, so
\sum_{j\in\mathsf{comp}(S)}w^{\prime}_{j}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}
is also
2\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}
-close to
\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}
. But if
\left\lVert\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}-I_{d}\right\rVert_{F}>4\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}
, then by the triangle inequality
\sum_{j\in\mathsf{comp}(S)}w^{\prime}_{j}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}
has distance more than
2\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}
from
I_{d}
, which is a contradiction.
Therefore, for the rest of the proof we assume that
\left\lVert\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}-I_{d}\right\rVert_{F}\leq 4\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}
for all
i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
.

Recall that all the components are
\Delta
-separated, so for each
i\neq j\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
at least one of the following holds:

•

Spectral separation:
\exists v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}v<\frac{1}{\Delta^{2}}v^{\top}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}v
or
v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}v>\Delta^{2}\cdot v^{\top}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}v
,

•

Relative Frobenius separation:
\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}-I_{d}\right\rVert^{2}_{F}>\Delta^{2}\cdot\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\right\rVert^{2}
.

We argue now that, because
\left\lVert\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}-I_{d}\right\rVert_{F}\leq 4\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}
for all
i\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
, there must exist two components with spectral separation.
By
Fact

5.21
, for two
i,j\in\mathsf{comp}(S)
we have

\displaystyle\lVert(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}-I_{d}\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 25\max\left\{\left\lVert\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}-I_{d}\right\rVert_{F}^{2},\left\lVert\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}-I_{d}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\right\}\cdot\max\left\{1,\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\right\rVert^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq 400\varepsilon^{-2/k^{2k}}\cdot\max\left\{1,\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\right\rVert^{2}\right\}

\displaystyle\quad=400\varepsilon^{-2/k^{2k}}\cdot\max\left\{1,\frac{1}{\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\right\rVert^{2}}\right\}\cdot\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\right\rVert^{2}\,.

If
\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\right\rVert^{2}>400\varepsilon^{-2/k^{2k}}\Delta^{-2}
, we have from the above that components
i,j
do not satisfy relative Frobenius separation with parameter
\Delta
, so they must be spectrally separated with parameter
\Delta
.
On the other hand, if
\left\lVert(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\right\rVert^{2}\leq 400\varepsilon^{-2/k^{2k}}\Delta^{-2}
, it means that

(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}(\Sigma_{i}^{\prime})^{-1/2}\preceq\left(20\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}\Delta^{-1}\right)I_{d}\,,

so

\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}\preceq\left(20\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}\Delta^{-1}\right)\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}\,,

so for any vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we have
v^{\top}\Sigma_{j}^{\prime}v\leq\left(20\varepsilon^{-1/k^{2k}}\Delta^{-1}\right)v^{\top}\Sigma_{i}^{\prime}v
, so components
i,j
are spectrally separated with parameter
\varepsilon^{1/(2k^{2k})}\Delta^{1/2}/\sqrt{20}\geq\Delta^{1/4}
.

Therefore there exist two components that are spectrally separated with parameter at least
\Delta^{1/4}
.
Then we apply
Lemma

5.25
with the assumption that we have a
(1-\varepsilon^{1/12})
-good partial clustering (so that the approximate isotropic position requirement of
Lemma

5.25
is satisfied), and conclude that with high probability the outputted list contains a
{(1-O(w_{\min}^{-2}\varepsilon^{1/(50000k)}))}
-good clustering.

Thus, in both the case of Frobenius separation and spectral separation, we produce with high probability at least a
(1-O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/(4k^{2k})}))
-good clustering.
∎

5.2.2
Proof of
Theorem

5.2

Note that all partial clusterings that are contained in
\mathcal{C}
in
Algorithm

2
at any point are naturally associated with a tree where the children of a partial clustering
\mathcal{S}
are the partial clustering
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
produced when we run the refinement algorithm on
\mathcal{S}
.
Also note that the number of clusters increases by one at each level and is bounded by
k
and thus the depth is at most
k
.

For correctness, note that by
Lemma

5.27
with high probability this tree contains at least one path from the root to a leaf such that at each level if the node is a
(1-\varepsilon)
-good partial clustering with
\varepsilon\geq\Delta^{-1}
then the next node in the path is an
(1-\varepsilon^{\prime})
-good partial clustering with
\varepsilon^{\prime}=O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/(4k^{2k})})
. Conditioning on the existence of a path from the root to level
i
, we can extend the path to level
i+1
with high probability by
Lemma

5.27
.
By taking a union bound over the
k-1
steps of
Algorithm

3
needed to extend the path from the root to the leaves, we have that this path will exist with high probability.
We can assume we start with
\varepsilon=\max\left\{O(\varepsilon^{*}),\Delta^{-1}\right\}
, such that with high probability the samples in
\mathcal{T}_{1}
have at most an
\varepsilon
-fraction of outliers.
Then, we have that at level
i
the partial clustering is a
\left(1-O\left(w_{\min}^{-2}\varepsilon^{1/((4k^{2k}))^{i}}\right)\right)
-good clustering when
\varepsilon^{-1}\geq f(w_{\min}^{-1})
is large enough, which we lower bound by
1-\varepsilon^{1/k^{(4k^{2})}}
for all
i\leq k
.

Thus for
(\varepsilon^{*})^{-1}\geq f(w_{\min}^{-1})
large enough there exists some leaf that corresponds to a candidate clustering in the final list
\mathcal{C}
with few outliers, as required by
Lemma

A.1
.
Then by
Lemma

A.1
we have that we output a
(1-O(kw_{\min}^{-1}\max\{\varepsilon,\Delta^{-\Omega(1/k)}\}))
-good clustering of the input samples.

We argue now that the loop in
Algorithm

2
terminates in
f(w_{\min}^{-1},\Delta)
iterations and that the resulting list of candidate clusterings has size at most
f(w_{\min}^{-1},\Delta)
for some function
f
.
For all
\mathcal{S}
which are not leaves in this tree (and thus have size strictly less than
k
), by
Lemma

5.27
their number of children is bounded by some function of
w_{\min}^{-1}
and
\Delta
.
Therefore, the total number of partial clusters ever included in
\mathcal{C}
is bounded by some function of
w_{\min}^{-1}
and
\Delta
, and since we process each of these at most once the number of total iterations is also bounded by some function of
w_{\min}^{-1}
and
\Delta
.

Furthermore, each iteration of the loop in
Algorithm

2
takes time at most
g(w_{\min}^{-1},\Delta)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(d)
for some function
g
, and thus overall the loop, and the entire algorithm, finishes in time
g(w_{\min}^{-1},\Delta)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(d)
.

∎

6
Clustering Mixtures of Identical-Covariance Gaussians

We define now a notion of separation for non-spherical distributions with identical covariances.
For identical-covariance Gaussian components this corresponds to total variation separation of
1-f(1/w_{\min})
where
w_{\min}
is the minimum mixing weight of any component in the input mixture (see
Fact

3.17
).

Definition 6.1

(Identical-covariance parameter distance)
.

We say that two distributions with means
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and identical covariance
\Sigma\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
are
\Delta
-separated if there exists a vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
\langle\mu_{1}-\mu_{2},v\rangle^{2}>2\Delta^{2}\cdot v^{\top}\Sigma v
.

We state now our main result for clustering mixtures of identical-covariance Gaussians.

Theorem 6.2

(Main theorem, identical-covariance components)
.

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a
d
-dimensional mixture of
k
Gaussians
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}N(\mu_{i},\Sigma)
with
w_{\min}=\min_{i}w_{i}
and
\Sigma\succ 0
.
Furthermore, assume all components
i\neq j
are
\Delta
-separated.
Also let
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
be a distribution satisfying
d_{\mathrm{TV}}(\mathcal{M}^{\prime},\mathcal{M})\leq\varepsilon^{*}
.
Suppose
\Delta,(\varepsilon^{*})^{-1}\geq f(w_{\min}^{-1})
for some function
f
.
Then, given
\mathrm{poly}(d^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},(w_{\min}^{-1})^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},\Delta)
i.i.d. samples from
\mathcal{M}^{\prime}
, there exists an algorithm that runs in time
g(w_{\min}^{-1},\Delta)\cdot\mathrm{poly}(d^{\log^{2}w_{\min}^{-1}})
for some function
g
and outputs with high probability a partition of the samples
\hat{S}_{1},\ldots,\hat{S}_{k}
such that, if we let
S_{i}
be the set of samples generated according to the
i
-th component, then with high probability (up to a permutation of
\hat{S}_{1},\ldots,\hat{S}_{k}
)
14
14

14

It suffices to take
f(x)=\exp(\exp(\tilde{O}(x^{2})))
and
g(x,y)=\exp(\exp(\exp(\exp(\tilde{O}(\min(x,y)^{2})))))
.

\min_{i}\frac{|\hat{S}_{i}\cap S_{i}|}{|S_{i}|}\geq 1-O(kw_{\min}^{-1}\max\{\varepsilon^{*},\Delta^{-\Omega(1/k)}\})\,.

Remark 6.3
.

By a recent result of Blanc and Valiant
[
10
]
that proved an equivalence between oblivious and adaptive adversaries, we can also generalize
Theorem

6.2
to the adaptive adversary setting.
See
Remark

5.3
for a more detailed discussion.

The proof uses the fact that, when the distribution is in isotropic position, the norms of the means are bounded by
w_{\min}^{-1/2}
, and therefore in directions of separation
v
the component variance
v^{\top}\Sigma v
must be very small.
In fact, the mixture is separable in all directions
v
in which
v^{\top}\Sigma v
is very small, so it suffices to find such directions in order to partially cluster the mixture.

In
Section

6.1
we give an algorithm that recovers a low-dimensional subspace containing directions of separation.
Then in
Section

6.2
we use this subspace-finding subroutine to obtain an algorithm that satisfies the guarantees of
Theorem

6.2
.

6.1
Finding Directions with Small Variance

Similarly to the case of centered components, we define an approximate notion of isotropic position.

Definition 6.4

(
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position, identical-covariance components)
.

For
\gamma\leq 1
, a mixture is in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position if we have that
\left\lVert\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\mu_{i}\right\rVert\leq\gamma
and
(1-\gamma)I_{d}\preceq\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\mu_{i}\mu_{i}^{\top}+\Sigma\preceq(1+\gamma)I_{d}
. We say that the mixture is (exactly) isotropic when
\gamma=0
.

Theorem 6.5

(Subspace finding theorem, identical-covariance components)
.

Consider a
d
-dimensional mixture of
k
Gaussians
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}N(\mu_{i},\Sigma)
with
w_{\min}=\min_{i}w_{i}
and
\Sigma\succ 0
in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position.

Let
\varepsilon>0
with
\varepsilon\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{160\log w_{\min}^{-1}})
, and suppose
\gamma\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{40\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\varepsilon)
.
Then, given
\mathrm{poly}(d^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},(w_{\min}^{-1})^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},\varepsilon^{-1})
samples from the mixture with an
\varepsilon
-fraction
of corruptions, there exists an algorithm that runs in time
f(w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1})\cdot\mathrm{poly}(d^{\log^{2}w_{\min}^{-1}})
and outputs an orthogonal projection matrix
Q\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
with
\mathrm{rank}(Q)\leq g(w_{\min}^{-1},\varepsilon^{-1})
such that with high probability, for every unit vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
that lies in the subspace of eigenvectors of
\Sigma
with eigenvalue at most
1/2
, there exists a unit vector
v^{\prime}
such that
Qv^{\prime}=v^{\prime}
and
{\lVert v-v^{\prime}\rVert\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1/8}\varepsilon^{1/(640\log w_{\min}^{-1})})}
.

Proof outline.
There are three main components to the proof of
Theorem

5.6
:

1.

In
Section

6.1.1
we construct a system of polynomial inequalities
{\mathcal{A}}
from the
exact

O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})
moments of the mixture that identifies the directions in which the variance is
\approx 1
.

2.

In
Section

6.1.2
we show we can construct a system of polynomial inequalities
{\mathcal{A}}^{\prime}
from the
approximate

O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})
moments of the mixture with the same properties as
{\mathcal{A}}
.

3.

In
Section

6.1.3
we argue that
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies the conditions of the rounding algorithm analyzed in
Theorem

4.2
.

At the end we will return to the proof of
Theorem

6.5
.

As a preliminary, we prove a property of generalized Hermite polynomials, which is used in our proofs.

Lemma 6.6

(Generalized Hermite polynomials)
.

Let
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}
be distributed according to a mixture of
k
univariate Gaussians
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}N(\mu_{i},\sigma^{2})
. Let
H_{e_{t}}(x,s)
be the
t
-th probabilist’s Hermite polynomial homogenized by
s
.
15
15

15

For example,
H_{e_{4}}(x)=x^{4}-6x^{2}+3
and
H_{e_{4}}(x,s)=x^{4}-6x^{2}s^{2}+3s^{4}
.
Then, for
s\geq 0
such that
s^{2}\leq\sigma^{2}
,
\mathbb{E}H_{e_{t}}(\bm{x},s)
is equal to the
t
-th moment of the mixture of
k
univariate Gaussians
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}N(\mu_{i},\sigma^{2}-s^{2})
.

Proof.

For
s=0
the conclusion is trivial.

Otherwise, for
\sigma^{2}\geq 1
, it is standard that
\mathbb{E}H_{e_{t}}(\bm{x},1)
is equal to the
t
-th moment of the mixture
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}N(\mu_{i},\sigma^{2}-1)
.
Then for
\sigma^{2}\geq s^{2}
we have that
\mathbb{E}H_{e_{t}}(\bm{x}/s,1)
is equal to the
t
-th moment of the mixture
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}N(\mu_{i}/s,\sigma^{2}/s^{2}-1)
, so
s^{t}\cdot\mathbb{E}H_{e_{t}}(\bm{x}/s,1)
is equal to the
t
-th moment of the mixture
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}N(\mu_{i},\sigma^{2}-s^{2})
.
Finally, the polynomial
H_{e_{t}}(x,s)
is equal to the polynomial
s^{t}\cdot H_{e_{t}}(x/s,1)
, so
\mathbb{E}H_{e_{t}}(\bm{x},s)=s^{t}\cdot\mathbb{E}H_{e_{t}}(\bm{x}/s,1)
.
∎

6.1.1
Identifying Eigenspaces with Large Eigenvalues Using Exact Moments

Definition 6.7
.

Let
\bm{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
be distributed according to the ground truth mixture. We define
{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)
to be the following system of polynomial inequalities in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

1.

\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
,

2.

\left(\mathbb{E}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2t}-(2t-1)!!\|v\|^{2t}\right)^{2}\leq\varepsilon\|v\|^{4t}
, for all
t=1,\ldots,10\log w_{\min}^{-1}
.

For ease of notation, we write
{\mathcal{A}}
without
v
or
\varepsilon
when understood from context.

We show now that
{\mathcal{A}}(v)
implies that the variance in direction
v
is very close to
1
.

Lemma 6.8

(Identifiability of large-variance directions)
.

Suppose the mixture is in exact isotropic position.
Then

\mathcal{A}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\{v^{\top}(I_{d}-\Sigma)v\leq O((\log w_{\min}^{-1})\varepsilon^{1/(20\log w_{\min}^{-1})})\right\}\,.

Furthermore, each monomial
r(v)^{2}\prod_{i\in S}p_{i}(v)
in the sum-of-squares proof has
|S|\leq O(1)
.

Proof.

Projecting
\bm{x}
onto
v
produces a mixture in which each component has variance
v^{\top}\Sigma v
.
Therefore by
Lemma

6.6
we have that
\mathbb{E}H_{e_{2t}}(\langle\bm{x},v\rangle,\sqrt{v^{\top}\Sigma v})
is equal to the
2t
-th moment of the mixture produced by projecting
\bm{x}
on
v
but in which each component has variance
0
.
Then, using that
\mathbb{E}H_{e_{2t}}(\langle\bm{x},v\rangle,\sqrt{v^{\top}\Sigma v})
is a degree-
2t
polynomial in
v
, we have for all
t\leq 10\log w_{\min}^{-1}
the polynomial identity

\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\mathbb{E}H_{e_{2t}}(\langle\bm{x},v\rangle,\sqrt{v^{\top}\Sigma v})=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2t}\,.

We aim now to also obtain another approximation for
\mathbb{E}H_{e_{2t}}(\langle\bm{x},v\rangle,\sqrt{v^{\top}\Sigma v})
given the constraints in
{\mathcal{A}}
.
If
\mathbb{E}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2q}
were equal to
(2q-1)!!\|v\|^{2q}
for each
0\leq q\leq t
, then the mixture obtained by projecting
\bm{x}
on
v
would match the first
t
moments of
N(0,\lVert v\rVert^{2})
, so by
Lemma

6.6
we would also have that
\mathbb{E}H_{e_{2t}}(\langle\bm{x},v\rangle,\sqrt{v^{\top}\Sigma v})=(2t-1)!!(\lVert v\rVert^{2}-v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{t}
.
We have instead the following approximate version:
{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}(\mathbb{E}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2q}-(2q-1)!!\|v\|^{2q})^{2}\leq\varepsilon\|v\|^{4q}
.
Therefore, using that
\mathbb{E}H_{e_{2t}}(\langle\bm{x},v\rangle,\sqrt{v^{\top}\Sigma v})
can be expanded as a sum of terms
C_{t,q}\mathbb{E}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2q}(v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{t-q}
with
{-(2t)^{t}\leq C_{t,q}\leq(2t)^{t}}
and that
0\preceq\Sigma\preceq I_{d}
, we can bound by
Fact

3.8
and
Fact

3.13

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}H_{e_{2t}}(\langle\bm{x},v\rangle,\sqrt{v^{\top}\Sigma v})-(2t-1)!!(\lVert v\rVert^{2}-v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{q=0}^{t}\left(C_{t,q}\mathbb{E}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2q}(v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{t-q}-C_{t,q}(2q-1)!!\lVert v\rVert^{2q}(v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{t-q}\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\varepsilon(t+1)(2t)^{2t}\lVert v\rVert^{4t}\leq\varepsilon(4t)^{2t}\lVert v\rVert^{4t}\leq\varepsilon(4t)^{2t}\,.

Putting these two observations about
\mathbb{E}H_{e_{2t}}(\langle\bm{x},v\rangle,\sqrt{v^{\top}\Sigma v})
together, we obtain that for all
t\leq 10\log w_{\min}^{-1}

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2t}-(2t-1)!!(\lVert v\rVert^{2}-v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{t}\right)^{2}\leq\varepsilon(4t)^{2t}\,,

so by
Fact

3.11

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2t}-(2t-1)!!(\lVert v\rVert^{2}-v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{t}\right\rvert\leq\sqrt{\varepsilon}(4t)^{t}\,.

In particular, as an upper bound we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2t}

\displaystyle\leq(2t-1)!!(\lVert v\rVert^{2}-v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{t}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}(4t)^{t}

\displaystyle\leq(2t)^{t}(\lVert v\rVert^{2}-v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{t}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}(4t)^{t}\,,

which raised to the power
10
gives for
t\leq\log w_{\min}^{-1}
that

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2t}\right)^{10}\leq(4t)^{10t}(\lVert v\rVert^{2}-v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{10t}+\varepsilon^{5}(8t)^{10t}\,.

(1)

As a lower bound, we get for
t\leq\log w_{\min}^{-1}
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{20t}

\displaystyle\geq(20t-1)!!(\lVert v\rVert^{2}-v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{10t}-\sqrt{\varepsilon}(40t)^{10t}

\displaystyle\geq(10t)^{10t}(\lVert v\rVert^{2}-v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{10t}-\sqrt{\varepsilon}(40t)^{10t}\,.

(2)

Putting the upper bound and the lower bound together, and using
Fact

3.8
, we get

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{20t}

\displaystyle\leq w_{\min}^{-9}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2t}\right)^{10}

\displaystyle\leq w_{\min}^{-9}(4t)^{10t}(\lVert v\rVert^{2}-v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{10t}+w_{\min}^{-9}\varepsilon^{5}(8t)^{10t}

\displaystyle\leq w_{\min}^{-9}\frac{(4t)^{10t}}{(10t)^{10t}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{20t}+\sqrt{\varepsilon}(40t)^{10t}\right)+w_{\min}^{-9}\varepsilon^{5}(8t)^{10t}

\displaystyle\leq w_{\min}^{-9}\left(\frac{4}{10}\right)^{10t}\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{20t}+w_{\min}^{-9}\sqrt{\varepsilon}(32t)^{10t}\,,

where the second inequality uses Equation
1
and the third inequality uses Equation
2
.
Finally, using that
w_{\min}^{-9}\cdot(4/10)^{20t}\ll 1
for
t=\log w_{\min}^{-1}
, we get for
t=\log w_{\min}^{-1}
that

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{20t}\leq 2w_{\min}^{-9}\cdot\sqrt{\varepsilon}\cdot(32t)^{10t}\,.

Then by
Fact

3.16
also

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2}\right)^{10t}\leq 2w_{\min}^{-9}\cdot\sqrt{\varepsilon}\cdot(32t)^{10t}\,,

so by
Fact

3.11

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2}\leq O(t\varepsilon^{1/(20t)})\leq O((\log w_{\min}^{-1})\varepsilon^{1/(20\log w_{\min}^{-1})})\,.

Finally, because the exact isotropic position ensures
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\mu_{i}\mu_{i}^{\top}+\Sigma=I_{d}
,

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}v^{\top}(I_{d}-\Sigma)v=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2}\leq O((\log w_{\min}^{-1})\varepsilon^{1/(20\log w_{\min}^{-1})})\,.

∎

Next, we prove an easy corollary of
Lemma

6.8
, showing that
{\mathcal{A}}(v)
implies that
v
is close to the subspace of eigenvectors of eigenvalue
\approx 1
of
\Sigma
.

Lemma 6.9

(Identifiability of approximate eigenspaces)
.

Suppose the mixture is in exact isotropic position.
Let
P
be the orthogonal projection to the subspace of eigenvectors of
\Sigma
whose eigenvalues are at most
1-\delta
. Then

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{20.53386pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{-7.21112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(1)}$}}}\hskip-20.53386pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[20.53386pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\{\|Pv\|^{2}\leq O((\log w_{\min}^{-1})\varepsilon^{1/(20\log w_{\min}^{-1})}\delta^{-1})\}.

Furthermore, each monomial
r(v)^{2}\prod_{i\in S}p_{i}(v)
in the sum-of-squares proof has
|S|\leq O(1)
.

Proof.

Consider the eigenvalue decomposition
\Sigma=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lambda_{j}s_{j}s_{j}^{\top}
. By
Lemma

6.8
, we have that
{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}v^{\top}(I_{d}-\Sigma)v\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}
where
\varepsilon^{\prime}=O((\log w_{\min}^{-1})\varepsilon^{1/(20\log w_{\min}^{-1})})
. Then

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(1-\lambda_{j})\langle s_{j},v\rangle^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\,.

Therefore we also have

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\delta\sum_{j:\lambda_{j}\leq 1-\delta}\langle s_{j},v\rangle^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\,,

so

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}v^{\top}\left(\sum_{j:\lambda_{j}\leq 1-\delta}s_{j}s_{j}^{\top}\right)\left(\sum_{j:\lambda_{j}\leq 1-\delta}s_{j}s_{j}^{\top}\right)v=\sum_{j:\lambda_{j}\leq 1-\delta}\langle s_{j},v\rangle^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}/\delta\,,

so

\mathcal{A}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\lVert Pv\rVert^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}/\delta\,.

∎

We also prove the converse: if
v
is in the subspace of eigenvectors of eigenvalue
\approx 1
of
\Sigma
, then
v
satisfies
{\mathcal{A}}(v)
.

Lemma 6.10

(Sum-of-squares feasibility)
.

Suppose the mixture is in exact isotropic position.
Let
P
be the orthogonal projection to the subspace of eigenvectors of
\Sigma
whose eigenvalues are at most
1-\delta
.
Then

\left\{\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1,Pv=0\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}\left(v,O\left(\left(w_{\min}^{-1}\right)^{40\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\delta^{20\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\right)\right)\,.

Furthermore, each monomial
r(v)^{2}\prod_{i\in S}p_{i}(v)
in the sum-of-squares proof has
|S|\leq O(1)
.

Proof.

For ease of notation, let
{\mathcal{C}}=\left\{\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1,Pv=0\right\}
.
We prove that
v
satisfies the conditions of
{\mathcal{A}}
.
First, we have trivially that
{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\{\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1\right\}
.

Second, note that
{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\{(I_{d}-P)v=v\right\}
. Consider the eigenvalue decomposition
\Sigma=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lambda_{j}s_{j}s_{j}^{\top}
. Then

{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}v^{\top}\Sigma v\geq(1-\delta)\sum_{j:\lambda_{j}>1-\delta}\langle s_{j},v\rangle^{2}=(1-\delta)\lVert(I_{d}-P)v\rVert^{2}=(1-\delta)\lVert v\rVert^{2}\,.

Then, using that
\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2}+v^{\top}\Sigma v=\lVert v\rVert^{2}
, we also get

{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2}\leq\delta\lVert v\rVert^{2}\,,

so
{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2}\leq w_{\min}^{-1}\delta\lVert v\rVert^{2}
for all
i\in[k]
.
Then, for all
t=1,\ldots,10\log w_{\min}^{-1}
, using also that
0\preceq\Sigma\preceq I_{d}
, we have by
Fact

3.13
the upper bound

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\mathbb{E}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\sum_{s=0}^{t}\binom{2t}{2s}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2s}(v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{t-s}(2t-2s-1)!!

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\sum_{s=0}^{t}\binom{2t}{2s}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2s}\lVert v\rVert^{2t-2s}(2t-2s-1)!!

\displaystyle=(2t-1)!!\lVert v\rVert^{2t}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\binom{2t}{2s}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2s}\lVert v\rVert^{2t-2s}(2t-2s-1)!!

\displaystyle\leq(2t-1)!!\lVert v\rVert^{2t}+\sum_{s=1}^{t}\binom{2t}{2s}w_{\min}^{-s}\delta^{s}\lVert v\rVert^{2t}(2t-2s-1)!!

\displaystyle\leq(2t-1)!!\lVert v\rVert^{2t}+(4t)^{t}w_{\min}^{-t}\delta\lVert v\rVert^{2t}

and the lower bound

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\mathbb{E}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2t}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\sum_{s=0}^{t}\binom{2t}{2s}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2s}(v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{t-s}(2t-2s-1)!!

\displaystyle\geq(2t-1)!!(v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{t}

\displaystyle\geq(2t-1)!!(1-\delta)^{t}\lVert v\rVert^{2t}

\displaystyle\geq(2t-1)!!\lVert v\rVert^{2t}-(2t)^{t+1}\delta\lVert v\rVert^{2t}\,.

Then

{\mathcal{C}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left(\mathbb{E}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2t}-(2t-1)!!\lVert v\rVert^{2t}\right)^{2}\leq(4t)^{2t}w_{\min}^{-2t}\delta^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{4t}\leq w_{\min}^{-4t}\delta^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{4t}\,.

∎

6.1.2
Identifying Eigenspaces with Large Eigenvalues Using Approximate Moments

In this section we show how to construct a system of polynomial constraints
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
with properties similar to
{\mathcal{A}}
when only having access to approximate moments. Before doing so, we state a result on the closeness of the empirical moments to the population moments.

Theorem 6.11

(Theorem 1.3 and Lemma 5.4 in
[
36
]
)
.

Given
\mathrm{poly}(d^{t},w_{\min}^{-t},\varepsilon^{-1})
samples from the mixture with an
\varepsilon
-fraction of corruptions, where
\varepsilon\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{2}/t^{2})
, there exists an algorithm that runs in time
\mathrm{poly}(d^{t},w_{\min}^{-t},\varepsilon^{-1})
and outputs symmetric tensor moment estimates
\widehat{M}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}},\ldots,\widehat{M}_{2t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2t}}
such that with high probability, for all vectors
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and all
1\leq q\leq t
,

\langle M_{2q}-\widehat{M}_{2q},v^{\otimes 2q}\rangle^{2}\leq O(t^{q}w_{\min}^{-q}\sqrt{\varepsilon})\cdot\left\langle M_{2},v^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle^{q}\,.

Furthermore, there exist degree-
O(t)
sum-of-squares proofs in
v
of these inequalities.

We obtain the following simple corollary for mixtures in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position:

Corollary 6.12
.

Suppose the mixture is in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position with
\gamma\leq\Omega(1/q)
. Then the same result as in
Theorem

6.11
holds with upper bounds

\langle M_{2q}-\widehat{M}_{2q},v^{\otimes 2q}\rangle^{2}\leq O(t^{q}w_{\min}^{-q}\sqrt{\varepsilon})\lVert v\rVert^{2q}\,.

Proof.

See the proof of
Corollary

5.15
.
∎

In
Lemma

6.13
we prove that we can construct a system of constraints
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v)
that is roughly equivalent to
{\mathcal{A}}(v)
.

Lemma 6.13

(Approximate moment feasibility)
.

Suppose the mixture is in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position, with
\gamma\leq\Omega(1/q)
.
Given
\mathrm{poly}(d^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},(w_{\min}^{-1})^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},\varepsilon^{-1})
samples from the mixture with an
\varepsilon
-fraction of errors, where
\varepsilon\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{20\log w_{\min}^{-1}})
, there exists an algorithm that runs in time
\mathrm{poly}(d^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},(w_{\min}^{-1})^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},\varepsilon^{-1})
and computes a system of polynomial inequalities
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)
of size
\mathrm{poly}(d^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}})
in indeterminate
v
such that with high probability

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}\left(v,O\left((w_{\min}^{-1})^{10\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)\right)

and

{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\left(v,O\left((w_{\min}^{-1})^{10\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)\right)\,.

Furthermore, each monomial
r(v)^{2}\prod_{i\in S}p_{i}(v)
in the sum-of-squares proofs has
|S|\leq O(1)
.

Proof.

We compute by
Corollary

6.12
in time
\mathrm{poly}(d^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},(w_{\min}^{-1})^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},\varepsilon^{-1})
some
\widehat{M}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
,
\ldots
,
\widehat{M}_{2t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2t}}
with
t=10\log w_{\min}^{-1}
such that for all
1\leq q\leq t

\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\langle M_{2q}-\widehat{M}_{2q},v^{\otimes 2q}\rangle^{2}\leq O(t^{q}w_{\min}^{-q}\sqrt{\varepsilon})\lVert v\rVert^{4q}\,.

Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}=O(t^{t}w_{\min}^{-t}\sqrt{\varepsilon})
.
Then we construct the following system of polynomial inequalities
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)
in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

1.

\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
,

2.

\left(\langle\widehat{M}_{2t},v^{\otimes 2t}\rangle-(2t-1)!!\|v\|^{2t}\right)^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\|v\|^{4t}
, for all
t=1,\ldots,10\log w_{\min}^{-1}
.

We start by proving that
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}(v,O(\varepsilon^{\prime}))
. We have trivially that
{\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1}
. For the second constraint, we have by
Fact

3.8

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}

\displaystyle\left(\langle M_{2t},v^{\otimes 2t}\rangle-\|v\|^{2t}(2t-1)!!\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\langle\widehat{M}_{2t},v^{\otimes 2t}\rangle-\|v\|^{2t}(2t-1)!!\right)^{2}+2\langle M_{2t}-\widehat{M}_{2t},v^{\otimes 2t}\rangle^{2}

\displaystyle\leq O(\varepsilon^{\prime})\lVert v\rVert^{4t}\,.

Therefore
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}(v,O(\varepsilon^{\prime}))
.

A similar set of inequalities gives the other direction.
∎

Finally, we show that even if the mixture is only in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position,
{\mathcal{A}}(v)
is still roughly equivalent to the system of constraints that we would have if the mixture were in exact isotropic position.

Lemma 6.14

(Approximate isotropic position)
.

Suppose the mixture is in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position, with
\gamma\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{20\log w_{\min}^{-1}})
.
Let
\tilde{\mu}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\mu_{i}
and
{\tilde{\Sigma}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\mu_{i}\mu_{i}^{\top}+\Sigma}
, and let
\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)
be the system of polynomial inequalities in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in
Definition

6.7
for the mixture
\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\bm{x}-\tilde{\mu})
where
\bm{x}
is distributed according to the ground truth mixture, that is, for the ground truth mixture put into exact isotropic position.
Then

{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-4.975pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{3.71112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}$}}}\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}\left(v,O\left(\varepsilon+\left(w_{\min}^{-1}\right)^{40\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\gamma^{2}\right)\right)

and

\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-4.975pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{3.71112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}\left(v,O\left(\varepsilon+\left(w_{\min}^{-1}\right)^{40\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\gamma^{2}\right)\right)\,.

Furthermore, each monomial
r(v)^{2}\prod_{i\in S}p_{i}(v)
in the sum-of-squares proofs has
|S|\leq O(1)
.

Proof.

We start by proving that
{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-4.975pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{3.71112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}$}}}\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}\left(v,O\left(\varepsilon+2^{O(t)}w_{\min}^{-2t}\gamma^{2}\right)\right)
.
We have trivially that
{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{8.25113pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{-6.47223pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{2}$}}}\hskip-8.25113pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[8.25113pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
.

For the second constraint, we have for all
t=1,\ldots,10\log w_{\min}^{-1}
by
Fact

3.8
and
Fact

3.7

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\bm{x}-\tilde{\mu}),v\rangle^{2t}-(2t-1)!!\lVert v\rVert^{2t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2t}-(2t-1)!!\lVert v\rVert^{2t}\right)^{2}+2\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\bm{x}-\tilde{\mu}),v\rangle^{2t}-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\lVert v\rVert^{4t}+2\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\bm{x}-\tilde{\mu}),v\rangle^{2t}-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=2\varepsilon\lVert v\rVert^{4t}+2\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}(\langle\bm{x},v\rangle+\langle(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d})\bm{x}-\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\tilde{\mu},v\rangle)^{2t}-\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2t}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\lVert v\rVert^{4t}+2^{O(t)}\cdot O\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{2t-1}\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{\ell}\langle(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d})\bm{x}-\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\tilde{\mu},v\rangle^{2t-\ell}\right)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\lVert v\rVert^{4t}+2^{O(t)}\cdot O\left(\sum_{\ell=0}^{2t-1}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2\ell}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d})\bm{x}-\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\tilde{\mu},v\rangle^{4t-2\ell}\right)\,,

where in the last line we used
Fact

3.15
.
Because of the
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position we have
\lVert\mu_{i}\rVert\leq w_{\min}^{-1/2}
for all
i\in[k]
and
\lVert\Sigma\rVert\leq O(1)
, and then we can bound for any
\ell
using
Fact

3.13

\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\bm{x},v\rangle^{2\ell}\leq 2^{O(\ell)}\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\langle\mu_{i},v\rangle^{2\ell}+\ell^{O(\ell)}(v^{\top}\Sigma v)^{\ell}\leq\ell^{O(\ell)}w_{\min}^{-\ell}\lVert v\rVert^{2\ell}

and similarly, using that
\lVert\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d}\rVert\leq O(\gamma)
and
\lVert\tilde{\mu}\rVert\leq O(\gamma)
,

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d})\bm{x}-\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\tilde{\mu},v\rangle^{2\ell}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{O(\ell)}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle(\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}-I_{d})\bm{x},v\rangle^{2\ell}+2^{O(\ell)}\gamma^{2\ell}

\displaystyle\leq\ell^{O(\ell)}w_{\min}^{-\ell}\gamma^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{2\ell}\,.

Therefore we get

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left(\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\langle\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\bm{x}-\tilde{\mu}),v\rangle^{2t}-\lVert v\rVert^{2t}(2t-1)!!\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\lVert v\rVert^{4t}+t^{O(t)}w_{\min}^{-2t}\gamma^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{4t}

\displaystyle\leq 2\varepsilon\lVert v\rVert^{4t}+w_{\min}^{-4t}\gamma^{2}\lVert v\rVert^{4t}\,.

A similar set of inequalities gives the other direction.
∎

6.1.3
Final Constraint Construction

In this section, we show that we can construct
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}
that satisfies the conditions of the rounding algorithm analyzed in
Theorem

4.2
.

Lemma 6.15

(Identical-covariance rounding conditions)
.

Suppose the mixture is in
\gamma
-approximate isotropic position with
\gamma\leq\Omega(1/q)
.
Let
{\mathcal{A}}(v,\varepsilon)
be the system of polynomial inequalities for the mixture.
Let
\tilde{\mu}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\mu_{i}
and
{\tilde{\Sigma}=\sum_{i=1}^{k}w_{i}\mu_{i}\mu_{i}^{\top}+\Sigma}
, and let
\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)
be the system of polynomial inequalities in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
in
Definition

6.7
for the mixture
\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}(\bm{x}-\tilde{\mu})
where
\bm{x}
is distributed according to the ground truth mixture, that is, for the ground truth mixture put into exact isotropic position.

Let
P
be the orthogonal projection to the subspace of eigenvectors of
\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}
whose eigenvalues are at most
1-\delta^{1/16}
.
Also let
R
be the orthogonal projection to the subspace of eigenvectors of
\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}\Sigma\tilde{\Sigma}^{-1/2}
whose eigenvalues are at most
1-\delta
.

Suppose
\varepsilon\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{160\log w_{\min}^{-1}})
,
\gamma\leq\Omega(w_{\min}^{40\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\varepsilon)
, and
\delta\sim\Omega(w_{\min}^{3}\varepsilon^{1/(10\log w_{\min}^{-1})})
small enough.
Given
\mathrm{poly}(d^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},(w_{\min}^{-1})^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},\varepsilon^{-1})
samples from the mixture with an
\varepsilon
-fraction of corruptions, there exists an algorithm that runs in time
\mathrm{poly}(d^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},(w_{\min}^{-1})^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},\varepsilon^{-1})
and computes a set of polynomial inequalities
\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
of size
\mathrm{poly}(d^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}})
in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, including
\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
, such that with high probability:

1.

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\{\left\lVert Pv\right\rVert^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/(80\log w_{\min}^{-1})})\right\}
,

2.

\left\{\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1,Rv=0\right\}\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}
.

Furthermore, each monomial
r(v)^{2}\prod_{i\in S}p_{i}(v)
in the sum-of-squares proofs has
|S|\leq O(1)
.

Proof.

By
Lemma

6.13
and
Lemma

6.14
we can construct a system of polynomial inequalities
\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)
in time
\mathrm{poly}(d^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},(w_{\min}^{-1})^{\log w_{\min}^{-1}},\varepsilon^{-1})
such that

\displaystyle\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}\left(v,O\left((w_{\min}^{-1})^{10\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\sqrt{\varepsilon}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\tilde{{\mathcal{A}}}\left(v,O\left((w_{\min}^{-1})^{10\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\left(w_{\min}^{-1}\right)^{40\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\gamma^{2}\right)\right)\,.

Let
\varepsilon^{\prime}=O\left((w_{\min}^{-1})^{10\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\sqrt{\varepsilon}+\left(w_{\min}^{-1}\right)^{40\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\gamma^{2}\right)
.
Then by
Lemma

6.9

\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\lVert Pv\right\rVert^{2}\leq O((\log w_{\min}^{-1})(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{1/(20\log w_{\min}^{-1})}\delta^{-1/16})\,,

where using that
\delta^{1/16}\geq(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{1/(40\log w_{\min}^{-1})}
we have the bound

\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\lVert Pv\right\rVert^{2}\leq O((\log w_{\min}^{-1})(\varepsilon^{\prime})^{1/(40\log w_{\min}^{-1})})\,,

where using the bounds on
\varepsilon
and
\gamma
we get the bound

\widehat{{\mathcal{A}}}(v,\varepsilon)\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}\left\lVert Pv\right\rVert^{2}\leq O(w_{\min}^{-1}\varepsilon^{1/(80\log w_{\min}^{-1})})\,.

For the second claim, we start by noting that, by
Lemma

6.10
, there exists a sum-of-squares proof in indeterminate
v\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
that, if
\lVert v\rVert^{2}\leq 1
and
Rv=0
, then
v
is feasible for
\tilde{\mathcal{A}}(v,O((w_{\min}^{-1})^{40\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\delta^{20\log w_{\min}^{-1}}))
.
Then by
Lemma

6.13
and
Lemma

6.14

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{A}}\left(v,O\left((w_{\min}^{-1})^{40\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\delta^{20\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{3.44444pt}{$\rule[-6.45831pt]{0.47787pt}{12.91663pt}\rule[-0.23894pt]{44.50748pt}{0.47787pt}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{-8.20114pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{O(\log w_{\min}^{-1})}$}}}\hskip-44.50748pt\raisebox{1.96112pt}{\makebox[44.50748pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{v}$}}}$}}}{\mathcal{A}}\left(v,O\left((w_{\min}^{-1})^{40\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\delta^{20\log w_{\min}^{-1}}+\left(w_{\min}^{-1}\right)^{40\log w_{\min}^{-1}}\gamma^{2}\right)\right)
